Decided by the user, 2026-11-24, at Tingyu's request: his DPO-trained judge (`Panreta:judge-api`, `explorer/train_dpo.py`) learns A/B preferences on rJokes upvote pairs built from joke + mode, and he asked for the room (venue, energy, age) as well. **Options:** (a) relabel the 90k R2 jokes with the room each suits best, using the same Qwen2.5-7B as R2 (`explorer/label_state.py`, about 5 h on one V100); (b) no relabel: put the room in the generator's prompt and let the judge rank our own generated candidates per premise, mode and room (the backup plan). **Choice: (a), with (b) kept as the backup** (user). **Expected limits, stated before the run:** the pair winner is decided by Reddit upvotes, which don't depend on the room, so a state-conditioned judge is predicted to score about the same as without state (Tingyu's reported 65%); the state labels are the LLM's guess, not measured in any room; label spread per field is checked before hand-over, and a field with over about 80% in one value is reported as carrying little information. The joined file holds rJokes text, stays on the cluster and is never committed (D-25, D-26 unchanged: the data trains a judge, not the generator). Vocabulary equals `sim.env` VENUES, ENERGIES, AGES (test), so labels match what the live loop sends. Judgment call, not measured.
**Result, Exp 25 (Explorer job 12123581, 2026-11-24, 8.8 jokes/s, 2.8 h):** 89,924 rows written, 76 left out (31 room-label parse failures, 45 earlier mode failures). **Every field fails the 80% check:** venue showcase 94.6% (bar 4.8%, company_event 0.6%), energy high 84.6% (normal 14.9%, low 0.4%), age young_adults 95.9% (mixed 4.1%, teens 1 joke). So the room labels carry almost no information for the judge, on top of upvotes not depending on the room. Part of it is the prompt: "showcase" was described as an audience that likes clever or odd material, which fits most Reddit jokes; a rebalanced prompt would still be the model's guess, so no rerun. Teens = 1 is expected for Reddit's adult material. Handed to Tingyu with these numbers; the backup plan (room in the joke writer's prompt, judge ranks our own candidates) stays the main route. Evidence: notebooks/decisions-data.ipynb Exp 25; metrics.md "Room labels for the judge's pairs".
## <a id="d-90"></a>D-90 · 2026-11-24 — A paused or cut-short show keeps its usable parts; pauses get log rows (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-24, while #59 (pause button) is open. A pause holds the show on the current line (the browser suspends playback and the server waits for `played`), so a show can run in parts, or end early if it never resumes. **Options:** (a) discard any paused show from training: cleanest, but throws away labeled jokes when there is only one live show; (b) keep the parts as today, with no pause rows; (c) **keep the parts and log `pause` and `resume` rows** (user). **What each use keeps (checked in the code):** the director already replays every bit with a `bit_end` row, aborted shows included (`app.director.finished_bits`), so finished bits train it and only a bit cut mid-way is dropped; every labeled joke stays usable for judge checks and DPO rows; the laugh detector keeps every clip once the pause is marked, because without a row `play()` returns only after Resume, so the paused joke's `audio_end_at` swallows the pause and room talk in it could be learned as "no laugh". **Reported outcome:** the room rating of a paused or aborted show is kept in the log but left out of the reported rating, since half a show isn't comparable (invariant 5). **Follow-up, after #59 merges:** the browser sends `pause`/`resume`, the server logs them with times on the show clock, audio alignment and joke spans skip the paused stretch, and the acceptance scorer reports pauses. A pause after the reaction window opened needs no special case: the window is server-side and keys still work while paused. Judgment call, not measured.


## <a id="d-91"></a>D-91 · 2026-11-24 — Exp 23: cached popular bits fill the show-open and between-bit silences (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-24; revised after review round 1 (before any run). **Problem (measured from `data/sessions`, 4 Gemini shows):** `show_start` to the first joke audio is **7.5 to 11.5 s**, because bit 0 has to be generated and pass the safety filter before its first line and `_bridge` ran only for bit > 0. A played line lasts 6.3 s on average (513 jokes, 55 bridges, same logs). **Options:** (a) leave it (a room hears 10 s of nothing at the start); (b) a generic open-up line before bit 0 (cheap, but the same ten lines every show); (c) cached popular bits: the best-received bits of earlier live shows played as filler (real material, picked by the room's own labels). **Choice: (c)** (user), with generic bridge lines kept as the second filler.
**What was built.** `app/cache.py`: `popular_bits(sessions, per_mode=2, min_labels=3)` takes bits from **live shows only** (a sim reward comes from made-up `audience.yaml` values, so it cannot define "popular"), recomputes each bit's reward from the reaction rows with `joke_levels` and `reward_v1` (never `bit_end.reward`), skips a bit with fewer than `min_labels` labels, no reward or no usable line, ranks by reward (ties to the later show), drops an exact duplicate of a better bit and keeps `per_mode` per mode. **A line is not usable when the bank played it** (the source show's `candidates` row has a bank generator id or a "hand-checked bank" reason: the bank can say it again as a joke, so it would repeat) **or when the room scored it level 0.** Each `CachedBit` carries the source show's age and venue. `run_show(cached=...)` plays them: **at the show open** while bit 0 prepares (the cached bit of bit 0's mode, else the best of any mode; its lines in order; at most `OPEN_CAP` = 3 lines, and only while `prep` is not done; rendered from the moment the show starts, and the show waits for a render only while bit 0 is itself still not ready), and **between bits** before the generic bridge lines (the next bit's mode preferred; `MAX_BRIDGES` = 8 caps a wait, cached lines and fillers together). **A line is never said twice in a show:** the pool is deduplicated by text across all bits. A cached line is **not evaluated**: no reaction window, no reward, no director update; it is written as a `bridge` row with `kind: "cached"` and `source` `{session, bit, joke, mode}`; the opener's rows use `bit=0`. Generic bridge rows carry `kind: "filler"`. The validator accepts rows without `kind` (older logs), rejects any other value, and a `cached` row needs `source`. Tests rebuild the director from a log with cached bridge rows and a `cache` row and from one without: A and b are identical.
**Safety (invariant 1).** A cached line passed the filter for its own show's audience. The re-check with the rules layer (`check_rules`, else `check`; no API call) for this show's age runs before the show starts and drops a failing line, but the **LLM teen rule never re-runs on a cached line**, so for a teen room (an age outside `safety.STANDARD_AGES`) only bits from shows of that same teen age class are used, and none otherwise; an adult room takes bits from either. A filter that raises fails the line. Bits from the same venue as this show go first. The bank stack's `BankOnlySafety` has no rules layer, and the bank stack gets no cached lines at all (`cached_for` passes `cached` only when the stack has bridge lines): it has no filler today, so a cached line before every bit would be new audio there and would queue its voice in front of bit 0's first render.
**Invariant 6 (log everything).** A `cache` row (schema 2, optional) is written right after `show_start` and lists every pooled bit `{session, bit, mode, reward, source_age, lines: [{joke, text, verdict, reason}]}`, including each line the re-check dropped and why (rules reason, "teen room, source show was X", "duplicate of a pooled line"). Replay ignores it.
**Voice contention.** Cached renders are one chain: the open's render task, then the rest (round-robin by mode, so a next bit of any mode with a cache finds a rendered line, at most `CACHED_RENDERS` = 15) chained behind it, so at most one cached render is ever asking the serial voice and bit 0's first line waits behind at most one. Aborting during the open cancels the waiter in a `finally`.
**Judgment calls, not measured:** (a) `OPEN_CAP` = 3: two lines (12.6 s) cover the worst measured wait and a third leaves margin for a slow provider; (b) `CACHED_RENDERS` = 15; (c) failing lines are dropped one by one rather than the whole bit, so a bit can be said with a missing line; (d) `min_labels` is applied on top of `reward_v1`'s own 3-label floor; (e) only one finished live show exists locally (neurotic up to 0.67, optimist 0.40, cynic 0.27), so a mode with no cache plays as before; (f) a same-venue bit leads even with a lower reward.
**Exp 23 bar, fixed before any run (user's shows; no key here), changed once after review and before any run:** on 3 simulated shows with `GENERATOR=llm` whose logged `cache` row held a bit of bit 0's own mode with a line that passed the re-check at the start, show-open silence (`show_start` to the first audio of any kind, cached or joke) is at most **2 s on every one**. A show qualifies by what it had at the start, not by what it played: a slow or failed cached render is a **MISS, not an exclusion** (the first version excluded shows that did not open with a cached line, which would have hidden exactly that failure). A show with no such cached bit does not count toward the 3 (so such a run is "not decided"). **Between-bit uncovered silence is reported beside it with no bar** (same measure as Exp 20's `silences`); Exp 20's bar (nothing over 2 s) still judges it. **Why 2 s:** the wait is the first cached line's render, and a short line renders well inside that. **Number that would change the decision:** an open silence above 2 s on a qualifying show means the first cached line is not ready in time, and the fix is to render it before `show_start` rather than to drop the cache. Results: `sim/evaluate_cached.py`, `reports/metrics/cached_bits.json`, `notebooks/decisions-live.ipynb` Exp 23 (not run yet). **Numbering:** this is the next ID after main's D-89 at merge time; open PR #61 and the Exp 24 branch also claim D-89/D-91, so it may need renumbering again.

## <a id="d-92"></a>D-92 · 2026-11-24 — A joke whose playback overlapped a pause loses its label from the reward (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-24, extending D-90 (pauses get log rows). **Problem:** a pause freezes playback mid-line, so the operator hears a line in two parts, with a gap, while the room talks. The label pressed afterwards is a reaction to a broken delivery, not to the joke. **Options:** (a) keep the label: simplest, but the director learns that the mode playing at the time of a pause is a worse mode, and pauses are not random (the operator pauses when something is wrong in the room); (b) **drop that one label from the reward, as if the operator had not answered** (user); (c) drop the whole bit's reward: throws away the other four good labels over one interruption. **Choice (b).** `reward_v1` already needs at least 3 of a bit's 5 labels, so one paused joke leaves a reward from the other 4 and three paused jokes leave none. **Rule:** a joke is dropped when its `joke_played` audio span `[audio_start_at, audio_end_at]` shares time with a pause interval, touching at one instant does not count. A pause during the reaction window, the hold, a bridge or a cached line drops nothing (the span is audio only), because **the show holds while paused**: no line, bridge or cached line starts until Resume (`Pauses.running()` is awaited right before each `audio_start_at` is read), and the reaction window and the hold stop their timers while paused and run on with the time that was left (`Pauses.get_within`), so a key pressed during or after the pause still counts. Without this the next line would be sent while paused, play only after Resume, and lose its label though it played whole. A pause with no resume (the show ended paused) runs to the end of the show. **One rule, two callers:** `app.pauses.overlaps` is used by the live loop (`_joke` returns `None` for the reward the moment the line ends) and by the replay (`app.director.joke_levels`), and a test runs a show with pauses and checks the live `bit_end.reward` equals the replayed one and the online director equals the replayed one. **What stays in the log:** the `reaction` row with the pressed level, unchanged, so nothing is lost for later analysis; only the reward and `bit_end.n_labels` ignore it. **Laugh detector:** a paused joke's audio is excluded from its training set (the clip is `joke_played` span plus the reaction window; `app.pauses.paused_jokes(rows, bit)` names the jokes), because the span contains the pause and room talk. **Pause rows:** `pause` carries `paused_at` and `resume` carries `resumed_at`, both on the show clock (the same timebase as `audio_start_at`, so `paused_at - show_start.audio.start_at` is the offset in the WAV; the recording keeps running while paused, so the WAV spans the pause and the acceptance scorer does not shorten its expected span). The server writes them after checking order (`app.protocol.check_pause`); `resume` without a pause, a second `pause` and either with no show running are an `error`, and the show continues. **Acceptance scorer:** reports per show the pause count, paused seconds and jokes dropped, and leaves the rating of a show with a pause, or of an aborted show, out of the reported rating while listing it (D-90). **Accepted limits (no code change):** a pause pressed within milliseconds of a line ending can drop that line, because the server stamps the pause before it stamps `end`; and live and replay agree only while the server clock does not step backwards (`time.time`, the log's own timebase). A pause is refused once `run_show` has returned or raised (`live.running`), and the scorer ignores rows after `show_end`. The browser undoes a pause only on an `error` marked `about: "pause"`. The acceptance key-bias check (D-90's operator check) counts the operator's raw presses, so a dropped label still counts there. **Numbering:** Exp 24's branch also claims D-92; whichever merges second renumbers. **Cost:** one label per paused joke, and a bit with 3 or more paused jokes yields no update. The paused label would have been honest about the room's mood for part of the time; (b) accepts that loss to keep pauses from steering the director. **Not measured:** Judgment call, not measured. No experiment, since the effect depends on how often the operator pauses, which the first real shows will say; the logged `pause` rows and per-show counts are the evidence to review then.

## <a id="d-93"></a>D-93 · 2026-11-24 — Model settings are named by job, and the joke writer can run on Claude through the official SDK (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-24, before the generator comparison (Exp 24, a later entry) so that comparison has something to compare. **Judgment call, not measured:** nothing has run against the real Claude API (no paid key on the build machine); the client is tested against a fake SDK only.

**Settings by job.** One model used to serve every purpose (`LLM_MODEL`, `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_API_KEY`, `GENERATOR`). Options: (a) keep one set of names and add `LLM_PROVIDER`; **(b, chosen) name each setting by the job it does** (`backend/sample.env`): `GEMINI_BASE_URL`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `JOKE_SOURCE`, `JOKE_WRITER_PROVIDER` / `JOKE_WRITER_MODEL`, `SAFETY_CHECK_PROVIDER` / `SAFETY_CHECK_MODEL`, `TRANSCRIPT_PIPELINE_MODEL`. Why: the live loop already has two model jobs (writing the jokes, judging each line before the voice) that must be able to differ, and a single `LLM_PROVIDER` would let a change meant for the writer move the safety check with it. **Each role reads only its own names**, so the safety check never follows the joke writer to another provider. Older `.env` files keep working: the old names are read when the new one is unset (`llm.LEGACY`). `LLM_PROVIDER` never shipped and is not a legacy name; a legacy model name (`LLM_MODEL`) only applies when no new name picked the provider, and is never used for Claude; Claude defaults to `claude-haiku-5-5`; a `claude-*` model on the gemini provider (or a non-Claude model on the anthropic provider) is a clear `RuntimeError` naming the fix, so a half-renamed `.env` cannot send a Claude model name to the Gemini endpoint. `backend/.env.example` is replaced by `backend/sample.env`.

**The Claude client.** `backend/app/llm_anthropic.py`, the official `anthropic` SDK (2.14.0), not an OpenAI-compatible shim. Options: (a) Anthropic's OpenAI-compatibility endpoint through the existing `llm.Client` (no new dependency, but it ignores `output_config` and `stop_reason` handling, and a refusal arrives as ordinary text); **(b, chosen) the SDK in its own module** with the same `chat` surface, `LLMError` and bounded retry loop as `llm.Client`. The loop is `llm.run_with_retries`, shared by both clients, and carries all of D-88's semantics: `validate=` re-asks once with no backoff, a re-ask is not a `retried`, a failed re-ask returns the first bad reply, `bad_replies` is counted. So the safety check can run on either provider, and `llm.Client` now counts tokens from the reply's `usage` (for cost) without that bookkeeping ever failing a good reply. What the client encodes: it never sends `temperature` (Haiku 5.5 answers a non-default one with a 400); `output_config={"effort": "low"}` (the default is medium with adaptive thinking on, which costs latency); structured output when the caller passes its reply schema as `json_reply` (a dict: `{"effort": "low", "format": {"type": "json_schema", "schema": ...}}`, the shape in SDK 2.14.0's `OutputConfigParam`; the generator and the filter each pass theirs, `generate.REPLY_SCHEMA` and `safety.REPLY_SCHEMA`; a bare `True` sends none); no prefill; `max_tokens` 4096 because thinking tokens count against it; the SDK's own retries off (`max_retries=0`, they would run outside the deadline); `anthropic.Timeout(t, connect=min(2, t))`; `stop_reason` checked before content (`refusal` and `max_tokens` raise a non-retryable `LLMError`, anything but `end_turn` is an error); text from `type == "text"` blocks only; errors caught most-specific-first (`RateLimitError` retryable, other `APIStatusError` retryable at 408 and 500 and up, `APITimeoutError` and `APIConnectionError` retryable); and **any other exception, including the SDK's bare `TypeError` for a missing key, becomes a non-retryable `LLMError`**, so the generator's error handling holds. The key is the SDK's own (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), a paid key the user adds to `backend/.env`. Selected by `JOKE_WRITER_PROVIDER=anthropic` (and `SAFETY_CHECK_PROVIDER` for the filter, which should stay on Gemini for now); the default stays Gemini, so nothing changes until the user chooses.

**Not measured, said plainly.** Whether Claude's replies parse as often as Gemini's, its real latency and cost, and whether the first real call reveals a parameter the SDK or model rejects: the first such call is the test. `scripts/latency_probe.py` refuses a Claude model (it speaks the OpenAI-compatible protocol).

## <a id="d-94"></a>D-94 · 2026-11-24 — Exp 24: compare Flash-Lite, Flash and Claude Haiku 5.5 as the bit generator (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-24. The live generator is Gemini `gemini-flash-lite-latest` on the free tier. The user wants the models available to them compared before picking one. **Candidates:** Gemini Flash-Lite (baseline), Gemini Flash (`gemini-flash-latest`), Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`, $0.10 in / $0.50 out per MTok). **Funniness is the user's blind vote;** latency, failures, safety pass rate and cost are measured. **Not decided here:** which model wins. The run needs the user's keys and votes, so this entry fixes the bar and the method before any call. **The bar was changed after review (items A to E below, E revised once more before any data), before any data existed;** the first version (failure rate at most 5% over 30 calls, p95 over successes only, prompts dropped when a model had no safe thread) is superseded and was never run. **D (user, same round): the failure and latency gates are measured on 60 distinct prompts, 30 of which are voted on. E (user, same round): a judge calibrated on the user's votes may add its rankings of prompts 31 to 60 (below).**

**Method (`sim/compare_generators.py`).** **60 prompts, the same for every model, one attempt each** (`N_PROMPTS`), a prefix-stable seeded list: modes round-robin (10 per mode; the first 30 have 5 per mode), venue, energy and age drawn from `sim.env` with no repeated combination per mode. **The first 30 are the ballot prompts (`N_BALLOT`) and are voted on; prompts 31 to 60 count only toward the failure rate, the latency p95, the safety pass rate and the cost.** (First draft: 30 prompts with one attempt, then 30 prompts twice per model; both superseded before any data. Repeats of one prompt share failure causes, such as a prompt that tends to produce malformed JSON or a slow reply, so their failures are not independent; 60 distinct prompts give about 60 independent trials for the same number of requests and the same voting load.) Each prompt is one bit generated by `generate.GeminiGenerator` with the production timeout, retries and deadline (10 s per attempt, 12 s over all, 2 retries): one call, 3 threads of 5 jokes. The prompt is the first bit of a show (`premises_already_told` empty), so it is identical across models; a long show's premise memory is not exercised. The same safety filter judges all 15 lines of every model, at its production timing (12 s per attempt, 25 s over all, 3 retries, `FILTER_TIMING` and `FILTER_RETRIES`) and always on Gemini Flash-Lite, so a pass-rate gap is the generator's. **The models differ in more than their weights:** Gemini writes at temperature 0.9 (the generator's setting), Claude at its fixed default, which it will not let us change. Recorded per attempt: wall latency over all retries (what a show waits), success or failure with a reason and a kind (`config`, `infra`, `model`), requests, tokens, per-line verdicts. Requests (180 generations and as many filter calls, about 50 minutes unattended) are paced per quota (7 s between Gemini requests, 1 s for Claude; the Flash-Lite generator and the filter share one quota), outside the timed call. Everything is cached under gitignored `data/model_comparison/gen-<digest>/` (digest of the generator and filter prompts, timing, retries, filter model and seed, so editing any starts a fresh run), resumable. **A file per model and prompt holds every attempt, appended, never overwritten.** `--redo-failed` adds an attempt only where the last one failed for a `config` or `infra` reason (a key, a setting or an unavailable provider); a `model` failure (a refusal, a cut-off, a reply not in the asked shape) is the model's answer and stands. On a model's first call of a run, a config failure (a 4xx that is not 408 or 429, including Gemini's 400 for a bad key, a 404, a missing Claude key, or any exception that is not an `LLMError`) stops the run and caches nothing; later such failures are data. A filter outage is retried on resume (up to 3 times) and never counted as rejections. The ballot key lives in `data/model_comparison/key/`, apart from the ballot and votes.

**Vote: ranking, not ratings.** Options: (a) rate each thread 1 to 5 (a user's scale drifts over 30 minutes and models cluster at 3 to 4); **(b, chosen) rank the models per prompt, best first.** Ranking is paired within a prompt by construction, removes scale drift, and is faster to answer than three ratings. Cost: it loses "all three were bad", and ranks are an ordinal summary. One thread per (prompt, model): the first thread whose five lines all passed the filter, which is what a show would play unchanged. **(C, user, after review) A model with no safe thread for a prompt is ranked last for it, and the prompt is kept:** a show would play the bank there, and dropping the prompt would hide the failure that matters. The ballot shows only the models that have a thread; with two shown the user ranks two, with one shown no vote is needed, and models without a thread share the last places (ranks 3, or 2.5 each, or all 2 when none has one). Letters are shuffled per prompt (seeded), the ballot shows venue, energy and audience but not the mode or any model name, and `export` is incremental so letters and votes never change once written (export after `run` has finished; a prompt not yet generated for every model waits). Voting is a terminal loop that saves after each answer. With `--models` naming two models the ballot has two letters, and scoring a subset of a three-model ballot re-ranks to the subset.

**Power: the 30 voted prompts detect only a large funniness gap.** The decision turns on the gap between the best and the next model, not on one model's own mean rank. With no real difference the sd of that gap is sqrt(2) = 1.41 ranks, so its 95% half-width is 1.96 x 1.41 / sqrt(n): **0.57 at 24, 0.51 at 30**, 0.46 at 36, 0.41 at 45 (one model's own mean rank is tighter, 0.29 at 30, which is not what decides and was quoted in the first draft by mistake). **Simulated through the real `decide()`** (Thurstone: one model g latent sd above the other two, score plus N(0,1) noise per model and prompt, best and second picked by observed mean rank, gap CI a paired bootstrap, 2,000 runs, all three models eligible; `compare_generators.simulate_decision_power`): at n = 30 the truly funnier model is called clearly funnier in **22% of runs at g = 0.5 (a gap of 0.4 ranks), 51% at 0.75 (0.6), 81% at 1.0 (0.8), 99% at 1.5 (1.1)**; at n = 45 about 36%, 73%, 94% and 100% at g = 0.5, 0.75, 1.0 and 1.5 (400 runs; the notebook table uses 300 runs and moves by a few points). So 30 prompts resolve a model that is clearly funnier by about 0.8 ranks and miss a subtle one; below that the bar's inconclusive branch applies and the cheaper model is picked, which is the intended outcome for a close call. 45 prompts would reach 94% at a gap of 0.8 ranks but is 1.5 times the voting load (about 56 minutes, over the 45-minute cap), so 30 stands. **Voting load (unchanged by the 60 generated prompts, only the first 30 are voted on):** at most 30 prompts x 15 lines (fewer when a model has no thread), about 1.25 minutes each (an assumption), about 38 minutes. Raise `--n` to add prompts (the list is prefix-stable).

**Bar, fixed before any run** (also docs/plans/live-loop.md, T13). **Eligible** = (1) **latency (B, user, after review):** p95 over ALL calls at most 12 s, the `generate.GENERATOR_TIMING` deadline (the bridge lines cover it, a longer wait is audible), a call that timed out, or failed after using the deadline up, counting as above it, so a model that often times out fails this bar; the percentile is nearest-rank over the 60 calls (the 57th value), so up to 3 timeouts are tolerated and 4 are not; AND (2) **failures (A, user, after review):** the 95% Wilson upper bound of the failure rate, over every attempt made, at most 15% (`sim.evaluate_safety.wilson`). Every attempt counts, including one that a later `--redo-failed` attempt succeeded after. Measured over **60 attempts per model, one per distinct prompt**, **up to 3 failures pass (upper bound 13.7%) and 4 fail (15.9%)**. Why 60: with 30 attempts only 0 failures passed (1 gave 16.7%), which ruled out a model with a true 3% failure rate 60% of the time. What the 60-attempt gate does (binomial at the cut of 3 failures; the 30-attempt rows are the superseded version): a model failing 1% of the time is ruled out 0.3% of the time (26% at 30), 2%: 3.2% (45%), **3%: 10.6% (60%)**, 5%: 35% (79%), **10%: 86%, so a 10%-failing model slips through 13.7% of the time (4% at 30)**, 15%: 98.5%, 20%: 99.9%. The reviewer quoted about 15% for the 10% case; the exact value is 13.7%. **These probabilities assume independent attempts,** which distinct prompts make closer to true than repeats would (repeats of one prompt share failure causes); the room contexts and modes are balanced across the 60, but a systematic cause tied to a mode or context would still be shared. Failures are calls that raised after all retries (429s, timeouts, 5xx, a refusal, a cut-off or malformed reply). **Among the eligible**, ranks are redone among them only (an ineligible model cannot split two others), the best mean rank is "best", and the paired 95% bootstrap CI of (next minus best) must exclude 0 for the best to win on funniness. **Judgment call:** the brief said "if the best's paired CI overlaps the next one's"; the paired CI of the gap is the right paired statistic (CLAUDE.md measure-before-cut, item 4) and is less strict than two separate per-model intervals not overlapping. Per-model intervals are still reported. Otherwise funniness is inconclusive and the cheaper of the two is picked (cost per bit from measured tokens), then the faster by p50. One eligible model: it wins. None: the generator stays as it is and the user is told. **What would change the decision:** four timeouts in 60 calls, or four failures in 60 attempts, removes a model; a rank-gap CI that includes 0 turns a funniness pick into a cost pick. The old first-version text claimed a p95 above 12 s over successes could rule a model out while timeouts were excluded from it; that gap is what B closes. **Cost** is estimated at paid list prices (Claude's from the user, and its output tokens include its thinking; **Gemini's $0.10/$0.40 and $0.30/$2.50 per MTok are assumed 2.5-generation list prices, unverified, and `-latest` aliases move**), so the Gemini cost column is a guess to check against the billing page; the free tier costs $0 and a show pays nothing today.

**Not measured, said plainly.** Whether the first fully safe thread shown on the ballot is as good as the thread a show would pick (a random one of the fully safe threads); the safety pass rate is one filter's view, not a human's; one voter, one sitting, 30 bits, one time of day, so latency reflects the providers' load then; temperature differs by provider (above). The Claude path is unrun against the real API: the first run may reveal a parameter the SDK or model rejects, which would stop the run on its first call (a config failure) or show as failures with a reason, and a fix would be a dev-log bug, not a negative result, until the implementation is checked. Judgment calls, not measured: 30 prompts, the first-safe-thread export, the pacing gaps, the assumed Gemini prices, the gap CI in place of overlap, the 15% and 12 s thresholds (decided by the user, not tuned on data); 60 generated and 30 voted prompts.

## <a id="d-95"></a>D-95 · 2026-11-24 — Exp 24: a judge calibrated on the user's votes may rank prompts 31 to 60 (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-24, after review and before any data. Extends D-94 (the comparison and the user's blind vote on prompts 1 to 30). The judge adds rankings of prompts 31 to 60 only if it first proves it agrees with the user.

**E (user, same round, before any data; revised after the judge's own review, still before any data): an LLM judge calibrated on the user's votes ranks prompts 31 to 60, but only if it first proves it agrees with the user.** The user ranks the 30 ballot prompts as planned. A judge learns their taste by example and ranks the other 30, so the funniness comparison can rest on up to 60 voted prompts. **Mechanism (`sim/judge_generators.py`; `explorer/judge_generators.py` runs it on the cluster):** the judge gets a few-shot prompt of the user's ranked prompts (the bits under the same blind letters the user saw, no model names, and the user's order) and ranks a new prompt the same way, replying `{"ranking": "BAC"}`. A model with no safe thread is ranked last, tied, exactly as on the user's ballot; a prompt with fewer than two bits shown is auto-ranked, not judged; **every prompt up to 60 must have a record for every model before the judge runs** (a missing record is never an automatic tie).

**Judge model (user decision, after review): Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct on Explorer** (`explorer/label_modes.py`'s transformers backend, float16 on the V100, greedy; `--judge-model claude-haiku-5-5` or `gemini-flash-lite` stay selectable and need their keys). Why: it is **neither candidate's family**, so no self-preference (a Gemini judge would favor two candidates, a Claude judge one), it is already cached and working on Explorer, and it costs no API money. It is a 7B model reading 12 to 20k tokens of few-shot examples, so its taste-learning may be weak: the agreement bars below are what decide, not the choice. (Earlier draft chose Haiku 5.5, about $0.25, and rejected Gemini Flash-Lite for per-minute token limits and Flash for latency; superseded.)

**Ollama option (user decision, after review, before any data): first try `qwen3.8:27b`, with Qwen2.5-7B (transformers) as the fallback default.** `--judge-provider ollama --judge-model qwen3.8:27b` (the model defaults to that). Why this model: it is the user's pick from Ollama's local list; **neutral family** (Alibaba; the candidates are Gemini and Claude); large enough that it plausibly tracks taste better than a 7B reading 15k tokens of examples; 4-bit it is about 17 GB and fits one V100 (32 GB) with room for a 32k context. **Its quality is unverified; the three agreement bars decide, not this choice.** Fallbacks, in order: muse-glimmer 30b (Meta), then Qwen2.5-7B on transformers (already working on Explorer). **How it runs:** Ollama serves an OpenAI-compatible `/v1` (`http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1`, no key), so the laptop path is the same `llm.Client` (no new client) when a server is reachable, and the Explorer path is `explorer/judge_ollama.sbatch` on the same bundle (Ollama unpacked without root under `$COURSE_DIR/ollama`, the ~17 GB model in `$COURSE_DIR/ollama/models`, `ollama serve` in the background with `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=32768`, the job fails at once if `ollama ps` does not show the GPU, then `explorer/judge_generators.py --backend ollama`). **Context guard (must): Ollama truncates a prompt silently to its context window, and our prompts are 12 to 20k tokens.** Three layers: the server is started with the context; the judge refuses to run if any prompt's estimated size (chars / 3.5, a conservative over-estimate) exceeds the configured context; and every reply's `prompt_eval_count` (`usage.prompt_tokens` on `/v1`) must be at least half the estimate, else the call is truncation, an infrastructure failure that is never cached (the run is `incomplete`). A per-call nonce at the start of the system message stops Ollama reusing a cached prefix, which would make that count look like truncation (the cost is re-reading the examples every call: a 27B 4-bit prefill of 15k tokens on a V100 is probably tens of seconds, so 180 calls is a few hours: an estimate, not measured). Thinking models: `think: false` is requested and any `<think>...</think>` block is dropped before parsing. **Not verified here:** that the Ollama Linux build supports the V100 (compute 7.0) and the cluster's CUDA <= 12.3 driver, that the compute nodes can reach Ollama's registry for the 17 GB pull, that `qwen3.8:27b` fits and runs at a usable speed there; the job is written to fail loudly rather than run on the CPU. Which tarball name the current release uses is also unchecked (the script tries `.tgz`, then `.tar.zst`).

**Position bias: one order per position (`latin-rotation-v1`).** Each prompt is judged under `s` orders when `s` bits are shown (three, normally): one seeded shuffle of the bits, then each cyclic rotation, so every bit is shown first, second and last exactly once and a judge's liking for a position cancels in the mean rank. Alternative: two orders, the second the reverse of the first (a third fewer calls; balances first and last but leaves the middle bit always in the middle, and an LLM's middle-position bias is not zero). Cost: about 180 calls (90 leave-one-out, 90 for prompts 31 to 60), still a few hours at most on one V100. A test checks on every prompt that the first-shown bit differs in every order and each bit takes each position once. Position bias is reported (how often the first-shown bit is ranked top, against 1 / bits shown); with full rotation a judge that always picks the first bit ends up with every bit tied.

**Leave-one-out agreement check.** Each of the user's ranked prompts is judged seeing only the user's OTHER rankings (29 of 30; a test checks the held-out prompt is never in its own examples). **Bar, fixed before any data; all three must hold for the judge's rankings of prompts 31 to 60 to join the user's 30 votes (60 voted prompts), otherwise the user's 30 votes decide alone and the judge is reported as unreliable:** (1) **top pick:** the judge's top pick matches the user's at least 60% of the time (credit 1/k for a k-way tie; an unjudged prompt counts as a miss) AND the Wilson 95% interval's lower end is above chance, the mean of 1 / bits shown (33% with three); (2) **pairwise order (added after review):** the share of bit pairs the judge orders as the user did (a judge tie earns half) has a Wilson 95% lower end above 50%; pairs of one prompt are not independent, so this interval is optimistic and the test is a second look at the whole order, not an independent one; (3) **per-model bias (the reviewer's addition, open to the user's veto):** per model, the mean paired difference between the judge's rank and the user's rank, bootstrap 95% CI over prompts, must not lie beyond 0.3 ranks on either side. 0.3 ranks is the reviewer's choice, a judgment call. **What check 3 guarantees:** no model whose gap CI lies clear of 0.3 passes; a tilt smaller than the CI's width (about 0.4 ranks wide with 30 prompts) can pass.

**Residual risk, said plainly (B, user: pooling stays if the bars pass).** The reviewer's simulation showed that a judge tilted toward one model by about 0.5 ranks that agrees on 60% of top picks **passes the bias check about 70% of the time**, and pooling its votes then raised false "clearly funnier" calls, under no real difference between models, **from 1 to 3% to 18 to 27%.** (That simulation is the reviewer's; I have not rerun it.) A neutral family removes the main source of tilt (self-preference) but not the others (length, style, a preference for a mode or a bit shape); **30 leave-one-out prompts cannot certify the judge unbiased.** The decision this feeds is a funniness pick between two models that is already hedged by the cost fallback, and the user's own 30-vote result is reported beside the pooled one (`votes_user_only`), so a pooled "clearly funnier" that the user-only table does not support is visible.

**Completeness and freshness.** A run in which any call was not answered for an outage, a bad key or a missing reply is **`incomplete`**: the gate is not decided, nothing joins, and `cg.judge_ranks` ignores it. The first-call config guard (a 400, 401, 404 or an unexpected exception stops the run, caching nothing) is only used up by a call that reached the judge, never by a cache hit. A result is **stale** (ignored, reported as such) if the user's votes, the number of prompts, the judge's prompt or the order scheme changed after it ran. Replies are cached on disk under the run folder (gitignored), each file named by a hash of the judge, its exact system and user messages (including the bits being ranked), so a changed vote, example or bit asks again; an infrastructure failure is never cached as an answer. The result file holds numbers and prompt ids only. Pacing for an API judge uses the provider's gap; prompt caching of the shared examples would cut an API judge's cost and is future work, not done.

**Explorer workflow (the user runs cluster jobs; Claude wrote the code).** `compare_generators judge --export-bundle <dir>` writes `calls.jsonl` (id, system, user, letters: our own generated jokes only, no rJokes, no model names, no key file; the letters-to-model manifest stays in the run folder) and `--smoke 3` writes only the calls of the first three prompts; upload it, `sbatch explorer/judge_generators.sbatch` (resumable and checkpointed like `label_modes`, batch size 1, SDPA attention since V100 has no flash-attn, an out-of-memory call split then skipped, a reply that does not parse asked once more with a nudge and otherwise left for the next run, never written as an answer), download `replies.jsonl`, `judge --import-replies <file>` scores it. Replies for ids from another bundle are ignored and counted.

**What the tests do and do not show.** `FakeJudge` ranks by the tag the fake generator put in each bit's text, so the tests check plumbing and arithmetic (the prompt, orders, cache, gate, bundle round trip), not whether any real judge has taste. The bias test uses noisy user ranks so the bootstrap interval has width.

**Power with the judge.** if the judge joins (it must pass all three bars), 60 prompts are scored: the gap CI half-width at 60 is 0.36 ranks (0.51 at 30), and through `decide()` the truly funnier model is called clearly funnier in about 46% of runs at g = 0.5 (a gap of 0.4 ranks), 88% at 0.75 (0.6), 99% at 1.0 (0.8) and 100% at 1.5 (1.1) (1,000 runs). **That is an upper bound:** the judge is a different rater that only approximates the user, its votes are correlated with the user's (it learns from them) and noisier, and 60 pooled votes are worth less than 60 of the user's own. The 30-prompt numbers above stay the honest ones for the user's own votes, and are what the decision uses if the judge does not join.

**Not measured, said plainly.** The judge may be a good mimic of the user on this one ballot and still be wrong about prompts 31 to 60 (different modes and rooms; 30 examples is a small sample of one person's taste); leave-one-out on 30 prompts with a 60% bar is a weak test (the interval is wide, so a judge agreeing 60% passes only if its lower end clears 33%); the bias check has the same 30-prompt width and will miss a small bias.
## <a id="d-96"></a>D-96 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 24: replace the Gemini writers with two local Ollama models, keep Claude Haiku 5.5 (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, **before any scoring**. Changes the candidates of D-94; the bar (gate, latency, ballot, pick rule) and the judge (D-95) are unchanged.

**What happened.** The first Exp 24 `run` hit a Google outage in its first minutes. Within 3 to 4 prompts Gemini Flash had **4 HTTP 503 failures** (already over the gate's limit of 3 in 60) and Flash-Lite had 3, while Claude Haiku had **3 of 3 OK (7.5 s)**. The user stopped the run. That folder (`data/model_comparison/gen-f036d862/`, gitignored) is archived, not scored: rename it to `gen-f036d862.aborted-gemini-outage-2026-11-25` (the user's data, not touched by the code).

**Options.** (a) Keep the data and resume: the 503s are real attempts under D-94 and stay in the failure count, so Flash is already over the limit before it has been measured in a healthy hour; resuming would measure an outage, not the model. (b) Restart the same three models after the outage: fair, but it keeps the live path's dependence on Google for the writer (a 503 sends a bit to the bank) and gives no way around it. (c) Change the rule so 503s on the same prompt do not count: moves the bar after seeing data, which D-94 forbids, and hides a failure a show would feel. **(d, chosen by the user) Replace both Gemini writers with two local models on the laptop plus Haiku:** `mimo-v2.6:9b` (Xiaomi) and `bonsai-2:4b` (Prism ML, ternary weights), through Ollama's OpenAI-compatible endpoint (`OLLAMA_URL`, default `http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1`, no key). Why: a local model has no provider outage, no quota and no price, and the writer would no longer depend on Google.

**What it costs.** (1) **No baseline of today's model:** Flash-Lite, the model a show runs on now, is no longer measured, so "better than today" cannot be read from this run, only "which of these three". (2) **Local latency is a near best case.** The comparison runs without the Kokoro voice. *Corrected in review, 2026-11-25:* Kokoro runs on the CPU (`backend/app/tts.py`, D-43), not the GPU, so it competes for CPU, not VRAM. A model Ollama keeps fully on the GPU is barely touched by it; one split onto the CPU would be, which is what D-97's gate rules out. What is left unmeasured is the small CPU overhead Kokoro's synthesis adds to a GPU-resident model's calls. (3) **The safety filter stays on Gemini Flash-Lite** (moving it needs Exp 19 re-measured; decide later), so it is still exposed to Gemini outages. A filter outage is retried on resume and never counted as rejections (D-94), so it can delay the run but not bias a candidate. (4) **Cost tie-break:** local models price at $0, so when funniness is inconclusive the rule (cheaper, then faster) favors local by construction; that is a property of the rule, not a finding, and the report must say so. (5) The candidates differ in more than weights: the local models write at temperature 0.9 through Ollama, Claude at its fixed default. (6) The ballot letters of the judge (D-95) now map to these three models; the judge's method and bars are unchanged.

**Mechanics.** Each local model is a `Spec` with its tag overridable by env (`MIMO_OLLAMA_TAG`, `BONSAI_OLLAMA_TAG`) in case `ollama list` shows a different tag; the generation digest includes every candidate's id, tag and base URL, so the new candidates start a fresh `gen-<digest>/` folder by themselves. Pacing: local calls are sequential and need no gap (the pacer interface is kept, gap 0). Local clients use the generator's production timeout (10 s), retries and 12 s deadline. **Thinking models:** both local models are tagged "thinking" on Ollama. `llm.Client` now strips `<think>...</think>` from every reply (a closing tag alone keeps what follows it; an unfinished block leaves an empty reply), which is generic and safe for Gemini, and local requests send `reasoning_effort: "none"` plus `response_format: json_object`. **Verified 2026-11-25 (Ollama 0.40.18):** `reasoning_effort: "none"` on `/v1` turns thinking off for both models (a one-line JSON reply: 0 reasoning characters and 6 output tokens, against 24 tokens for mimo and 159 for bonsai with the default); Ollama puts thinking in a separate `reasoning` field, not `<think>` in the content, so `strip_think` is a safety net only. `ollama list` shows the default tags (`mimo-v2.6:9b`, `bonsai-2:4b`), and the server runs at a context of 8192. **Config stop:** a connection refused on a model's first call is a config failure (nothing listening): the run stops, caches nothing and says "is Ollama running? `ollama serve`, and `ollama pull <tag>`"; a missing model (404) already stopped it. Start Ollama with `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=8192` (the generator prompt is about 2k tokens; this avoids a silent truncation at a small default).

**Evidence:** the aborted run's counts above (a few prompts during an outage: not a measurement of either Gemini model). Everything else is judgment call, not measured.

## <a id="d-97"></a>D-97 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 24: a local candidate must fit beside Kokoro; gemma4:12b joins as a reference (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, **before any scoring** (no candidate has generated data yet). Adds a term to the bar of D-94 and one model to the run of D-96. The latency and failure terms, the ballot, the pick rule and the judge (D-95) are unchanged.

**Candidates, updated by D-101:** the local candidates are now `bonsai-2:4b` and `qwen3:4b-instruct`; `mimo-v2.6:9b` was dropped after its 87% reading below, which stays here as the history of the gate.

**Problem.** The comparison runs without Kokoro, but a show runs Kokoro on the CPU (`backend/app/tts.py`, `device="cpu"`). A local writer that Ollama splits across GPU and CPU would use CPU the voice needs, and the comparison would never see that contention. A model's speed here is a best case anyway (D-96); this term removes the models for which it is clearly not achievable in a show.

**Decision 1: the gate.** A local model is eligible only if, **at context 8192, Ollama's own default placement puts it 100% on the GPU** (no `num_gpu` forcing). Measured from Ollama's `/api/ps` (`size_vram / size`) after the model's first successful call in `run`; the reading is stored in `placement.json` in the run folder (gitignored) with `size`, `size_vram`, `gpu_share`, `context_length`, `status` and a note. An "ok" reading is kept; an "unknown" one is retried on later successes (at most 5 per run) and on later runs. **Fails closed:** the status is "unknown", and the model not eligible, when the model is missing from `/api/ps`, the report is implausible (no size, `size_vram` above `size`, or `size` below half the file size in `/api/tags`), the file size cannot be checked, the server is unreachable, the model was loaded at a context other than 8192, or the reading is partial while another model was also loaded (the partial offload cannot be blamed on this model). 100% with others loaded is accepted: alone it can only be better. No tolerance: 99.9% fails. Each model's reason is stored ("reference", "partial GPU 87%", "placement unknown", plus the latency and failure terms) in the score output and in `reports/metrics/model_comparison.json`.

**Why fail closed.** Measured today, `/api/ps` is not always trustworthy: for `gemma4:12b` it reported size 1.01 GB, 100% on the GPU, for a file of about 8 GB (nvidia-smi showed 6.8 GB in use), and on a second try it listed nothing right after a successful generate. Cause unknown, suspected Ollama's reporting for gemma4 (dev-log). Trusting a missing or tiny report as "100%" would let a model through on a glitch; treating it as unknown costs a rerun at worst.

**Options for the gate, rejected by the user.** (a) **Allow a forced full offload** (`num_gpu=999`): `mimo-v2.6:9b` would fit (6.5 of 8.2 GB used, 36.5 against 33.9 tok/s) but with only 1.7 GB of headroom, so an out-of-memory failure mid-show is a real risk, and the show's own settings would differ from the defaults the user will have. (b) **Allow a partial offload:** the comparison runs without Kokoro, so it would not see the CPU contention it causes. **(c, chosen) Default placement must be 100% GPU.**

**Measured (2026-11-25, RTX 4060 Laptop 8188 MiB, Ollama 0.40.18, context 8192, nothing else on the GPU).** `bonsai-2:4b`: 1.94 GB, 100% on the GPU, passes. `mimo-v2.6:9b`: size 6.39 GB, 5.60 GB on the GPU = 87%, **fails**: it becomes a reference **by measurement, not by decision** (the registry does not mark it; the gate does, and a rerun on another machine or Ollama version could pass it). `gemma4:12b`: see above, unreliable report, not usable for the gate (it is a reference anyway).

**Decision 2: `gemma4:12b` (Ollama, pulled) is a reference.** It is generated, safety-checked, scored and shown in the stats table and **on the ballot**, but is **never eligible to be picked** (`Spec.reference`; tag overridable with `GEMMA_OLLAMA_TAG`, price $0). It cannot serve the live loop, so winning would be meaningless; it is there to show how far the eligible models are from a stronger writer. Options: (a) leave it out: no yardstick for how good "good enough" is; (b, chosen) a reference on the ballot; (c) let it be eligible: it fails the premise of D-97, since a 12B model does not fit beside Kokoro.

**What it costs.** (1) **The ballot has four options per prompt, not three** (letters A to D; `LETTERS`). Voting reads 20 lines instead of 15 per prompt: at the D-94 assumption of about 1.25 minutes for three, **about 1.7 minutes for four, about 50 minutes for 30 prompts, over the 45-minute cap D-94 set** (the 38 minutes of D-94 assumed three). **Cap raised to about 50 minutes (user, 2026-11-25); `--ballot` stays at 30.** That scaling is judgment call, not measured. (2) **The judge (D-95) sees four bits per prompt:** its few-shot prompts grow by about a third, so its context guard may now refuse a bundle (not checked), and its per-model bias check (D-95, check 3) now covers the reference too. (3) **Likely only two eligible models** (`bonsai-2:4b` and Claude Haiku 5.5), so the decision is a paired comparison of the two; if either fails the latency or failure bar the other wins as "the only eligible model" without a vote. (4) The local-first tie-break favors `bonsai-2:4b` by construction (D-96, item 4). (5) The vote ranks four, but the rule uses only the order of the eligible models: ranks are redone among them, so the references only cost the voter time.

**Power (computed, `compare_generators.simulate_decision_power(eligible=, references=)`, the real `decide()`, Thurstone model, 400 runs, 1000 bootstrap draws).** The power numbers of D-94 and D-95 assumed three eligible models. With two eligible beside two references the power does **not** fall; it is slightly higher, because the best model must beat only one rival, not the better of two. Chance the truly funnier model is called clearly funnier, at g = 0 (no gap), 0.5, 0.75, 1.0 and 1.5 latent sd: **30 voted prompts, three eligible: 0%, 23%, 50%, 79%, 99%; two eligible beside two references: 2%, 33%, 64%, 82%, 99%** (two eligible beside one reference: 3%, 33%, 54%, 79%, 98%). With the judge's 60 prompts (an upper bound, D-95): three eligible 1%, 47%, 87%, 99%, 100%; two beside two references 1%, 58%, 90%, 99%, 100%. The gap at g is in latent units: a gap of 1 sd is not the rank gap D-94 quoted (0.8 ranks was for three models; with two the rank gap is plus or minus 1 on every prompt, so the same g gives a bigger rank gap), which is why the tables are compared by g, not by rank gap. The no-gap fluke rate of a clear call stays at or under 3%. **Not measured:** how real votes behave, and the model assumes the references are ranked like any other model (the voter may be anchored by a much better reference, which could compress the gap between the two eligible).

**Not measured, said plainly.** Judgment calls: the exact-100% gate with no tolerance, the half-file-size plausibility test, the cap of 5 placement retries per run, the choice to accept 100% with other models loaded, and the 50-minute vote estimate. The placement of `mimo-v2.6:9b` at context 8192 was read once, in one session, with an unmodified Ollama; it may differ with the driver's free VRAM at run time. Whether `gemma4:12b`'s odd `/api/ps` report is Ollama's bug for that model or a setting is unknown. The reading is taken right after the model's warm-up (see the correction below); a partial reading while another model is resident is treated as unknown, so the user may need `ollama stop <model>` and a resume to get a clean one.

**Mechanics, corrected in review (2026-11-25).** Measured live on the user's laptop (Ollama 0.40.18): **Ollama keeps one of these local models in VRAM at a time.** Loading `mimo-v2.6:9b` evicted `bonsai-2:4b` (`/api/ps` then listed only mimo). Cold loads took **126 s for bonsai (the first call after the server started) and 35 s for mimo**, against a bit's 12 s deadline. The first build ran prompt by prompt (`for prompt: for model`), so every local call would have followed another local model's call and reloaded it: every local attempt would have timed out and both local models would have failed the latency and failure bars because of the harness, not the model. A show keeps one writer loaded, so that is not what a show sees. **Fixes:** (1) **Order:** each local model runs all its prompts in one block (`call_groups`), so it is loaded once; the hosted models stay together prompt by prompt, at the place of the first of them. Remote and local calls are therefore no longer interleaved in time, so a drift across the run (a provider's load at one hour against another, the laptop's heat) now falls on one model more than another: a stated limit, judgment call, not measured. An interrupted run no longer leaves every model with the same prompts; `export` still waits for all of them. (2) **Warm-up:** before a local model's first timed call in each run (a resume that makes a call warms up again; a fully cached one makes no call and does not), one untimed request goes through a client with a 300 s timeout and no retries. It is not an attempt: it is in no record, latency, failure count or token count, and its text is not kept. Its wall time is saved per model as `warmup_s` (with a history, one entry per run) in `warmup.json` in the run folder: the cold-load cost a show would pay if the writer were not preloaded (a number for the report). Nothing listening, or a missing model, in the warm-up stops the run as a first call does; any other warm-up failure is recorded and the run goes on. (3) **Placement is measured right after the warm-up,** when the model is resident and alone, with the fail-closed rules unchanged; the post-success retries stay for an unknown reading. **Verified live:** `measure_placement` against the real API gave `mimo-v2.6:9b` status ok, share 0.877, context 8192 (the field names, including `context_length`, are confirmed), and `bonsai-2:4b` measured after mimo was loaded gave "missing from /api/ps" (evicted), which is the fail-closed path working. A show should warm its writer when the server starts; that is suggested, not built. **Review round 3 (user-approved):** (a) at the start of each local model's block, a placement that is not ok is read again (warm-up, then `/api/ps`) even when every prompt is cached and no timed call is made, so a rerun of `run` after the server was restarted at a wrong context fixes it before `score`; the per-run cap of 5 still applies, an ok reading is never re-read, and each reading is logged in one plain line (passes, partial with its share and context, or unknown with the reason and what to do). (b) A warm-up that fails for a reason that is not a setting (a timeout) is retried once; a second failure stops the run with `WarmupError` and nothing cached for that model's block, instead of letting the first timed call absorb the cold load and count it as the model's failure. Both tries are in `warmup.json`'s history.

## <a id="d-98"></a>D-98 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 19 rerun: Claude Haiku 5.5 as the safety filter keeps the catch rate but misses the 3 s bar (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, after Gemini's outage (D-96): move the safety filter to Claude Haiku 5.5, but adopt it only if Exp 19 re-run on Haiku meets the same bars, fixed before any rule was written. The D-98 number is used although `main` ends at D-95, because PR #70 adds D-96 and D-97.

**Options.** (a) Stay on Gemini Flash-Lite: free tier, 1.9 s p95, but the outage and 29% 429/503 in Exp 19 showed the dependency. (b) Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`, $0.10 in / $0.50 out per MTok): already wired (`SAFETY_CHECK_PROVIDER=anthropic`). (c) Claude Sonnet 5.5: rejected before running, costlier and slower against a 3 s bar. (d) A local model: rejected, it would swap the writer out of the 8 GB GPU on every call.

**Bars (unchanged):** held-out hard-rule lines caught 100%; false rejects at most 10% on the bank and on the clean list, for adults and teens; at most 3 s added per call at p95. Same fixtures, same prompt `safety-llm-v2`, not touched.

**Bug found first (dev-log 2026-11-25).** The `safety_filter` cache key held only the fixtures and prompt digests, so setting the provider to Anthropic would have loaded the saved Gemini result. Provider and model are now in the key (`--provider`, `--model`, default from env), non-Gemini results go to `safety_filter_<provider>.json`, and the Gemini file only gained the two params (numbers untouched). The Claude client already honoured what the filter needs: `validate` runs in the shared retry loop, and `json_reply` is sent as a JSON schema in `output_config` (no `response_format` on Claude); no change needed.

**Result (1 run, 25 calls of 15 lines, 0 retries, 0 failed calls):**
| Bar | Haiku 5.5 | Gemini Flash-Lite (Exp 19) | Met |
|---|---|---|---|
| Held-out hard-rule catch | 71/71 (Wilson 95% CI 94.9% to 100%) | 71/71 | yes |
| Clean list false rejects, adults / teens | 2/60 (3.3%, CI 0.9% to 11.4%) / 3/60 (5.0%, CI 1.7% to 13.7%) | 1/60 / 1/60 | yes (point estimates) |
| Bank false rejects, adults / teens | 5/120 (4.2%, CI 1.8% to 9.4%) / 0/120 (CI 0% to 3.1%) | 4/120 / 0/120 | yes |
| Latency p95 per call | **4.94 s** (p50 3.09 s) | 1.91 s (p50 1.49 s) | **no** |
Rules alone missed one held-out line (`n18`, named_people); the LLM caught it. Clean rejects `k46`, `k57` (adults) and `k24`, `k41`, `k46` (teens); bank rejects `neurotic-1-3` and `absurdist-0-1` to `-4`, all adults only. The upper CI ends of the clean list (11.4%, 13.7%) are above 10%: the bar is judged on point estimates as in Exp 19, and 60 lines cannot rule out a true rate above 10%. Cost: 35,805 input and 15,519 output tokens, $0.011 for the run, about $0.003 for a show of 8 calls.

**Choice: not adopted. Defaults stay on Gemini; `sample.env`, the backend README and the status table are unchanged.** The latency bar is missed by 1.9 s at p95, and p50 itself is just over 3 s, with effort already `low`. Safety quality is not the problem. Per the rule fixed before the run, the prompt was not re-tuned and effort was not changed to chase the bar. Haiku stays selectable through `SAFETY_CHECK_PROVIDER=anthropic` as the fallback if Gemini is down; the live filter's budget (12 s per attempt, 25 s, D-88) would absorb 5 s calls, but each bit would wait that long before its first line and only bridge lines cover it.

**Not measured, said plainly.** One run: 25 calls make p95 the second-slowest call, so it could move; whether the slow calls are Claude's thinking tokens (15.5k output tokens for short verdicts suggests they are) or network is not isolated. A looser bar, a lower effort, or a smaller batch might pass; each is a new decision with its own measurement, not a retune of this one. The Exp 24 comparison runs the same filter on its own timing.

## <a id="d-99"></a>D-99 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 19 rerun: Claude Haiku with thinking off still misses the 3 s bar (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, after D-98: rerun the same check with Haiku's thinking turned off, the one change expected to cut the time, and adopt Haiku only if every bar is met. Bars, fixtures and prompt unchanged.

**Hypothesis, stated before the run:** D-98's 15.5k output tokens for 25 calls of short verdicts were mostly adaptive thinking, so `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` (accepted by Haiku 5.5 at effort `high` or below; we run `low`) would cut p95 below 3 s. The user asked whether a model with thinking off answers at random; it does not (temperature, not thinking, sets randomness), and the catch and false-reject bars are what would show any quality drop. Weak spot of those bars: only 6 soft lines, so a small drop on indirect digs could go unseen.

**Mechanics:** `llm_anthropic.Client(thinking=False)` sends `thinking: disabled`; effort and the JSON schema are unchanged (test). `python -m sim.evaluate_safety --no-thinking` caches to `safety_filter_anthropic_no_thinking.json` with `thinking: False` in the key, so it never loads or overwrites the thinking-on run; Gemini refuses the flag.

**Result (1 run, 25 calls, 0 retries, 0 failed):** held-out catch 71/71; false rejects clean 2/60 adults, 1/60 teens, bank 5/120 adults, 1/120 teens (all under 10%); **latency p50 3.12 s, p95 3.61 s: missed.** Output tokens 14,356 against 15,519 with thinking on (-7%), p50 unchanged (3.09 s with thinking on). **The hypothesis is refuted:** thinking was not the cost. The time is the visible reply, about 570 tokens per call: a verdict and a `reason` string for each of 15 lines. Cost $0.011.

**Choice: not adopted; the filter stays on Gemini.** The `thinking` option stays in the client (default on, so nothing else changes). **Next lever, not run:** a shorter reply (a reason only for rejected lines) would cut output tokens by most of the 570, but it is a prompt and schema change, so it needs the whole Exp 19 rerun on Gemini and Haiku both, and is the user's call.

## <a id="d-100"></a>D-100 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 19 rerun: the safety filter moves to Claude Haiku with thinking off, under a 7 s bar (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, after D-98 and D-99: "we can't do much with Gemini, it's a free tier and we might get 503s." The latency bar moves from p95 at most 3 s to **at most 7 s, with 5 s as the target** (the user gave "5 to 7 s"; 7 s is the bar, 5 s the target). Catch and false-reject bars unchanged.

**The bar moved after seeing the data.** D-98 (4.94 s) and D-99 (3.61 s) were measured against 3 s; the new bar was set knowing both. So they show Haiku fits under it, not that it passes a bar fixed in advance. The reason for moving it is a risk the 3 s bar did not weigh: on the free tier a 503 in an outage fails the check, fails the bit closed, and sends it to the bank (D-96 saw 4 of 4 and 3 of 3 failures in one outage).

**Options.** (a) Stay on Gemini, 1.9 s p95, with the outage risk. (b) Haiku, thinking on: 4.94 s. **(c, chosen) Haiku, thinking off: 3.61 s**, the same catch (71/71) and false rejects within the bar. (d) A shorter reply (a reason only for rejected lines): likely under 3 s, but a prompt change needing a full Exp 19 rerun on both models; not chosen. Between (b) and (c) the choice rests on speed: both pass at 7 s. Risk of (c), not measured: only 6 soft lines are on the list, so a small loss on indirect digs without thinking could go unseen.

**What it costs the show.** The check's p50 is about 3.1 s against 1.5 s on Gemini, so each bit waits about 1.6 s longer before its first line, inside the filter's 12 s / 25 s budget (D-88); bridge lines cover the wait. Not measured: whether Exp 20's between-bit bar (no silence over 2 s) still holds; the user's next simulated `GENERATOR=llm` shows (the Exp 23 runs) will show it. Money: about $0.003 a show. A Claude outage now fails checks the way a Gemini one did; Gemini stays one setting away (`SAFETY_CHECK_PROVIDER=gemini`).

**Mechanics.** `SAFETY_CHECK_THINKING=off` (new; `on` by default, a typo raises), read only by the safety check's role, so the joke writer never follows it (test). `backend/sample.env` ships Haiku with thinking off (test reads the file). The Exp 24 harness uses the same filter: `sim/compare_generators.py` pins Haiku without thinking for every candidate (not read from `.env`, so a run cannot switch filters halfway), drops the free-tier 7 s spacing between checks, and puts provider, model and thinking in the run's cache key (test). The comparison no longer needs a Gemini key.

## <a id="d-101"></a>D-101 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 24: send local models the reply schema, and replace mimo-v2.6:9b with qwen3:4b-instruct (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, **before any data** of the real run. Changes the local candidates of D-96 and the request the local client sends. The bar (D-94), the GPU gate and the reference (D-97), the ballot (four options) and the judge (D-95) are unchanged. (The IDs D-98 to D-100 belong to another branch, PR #71, not yet merged; `tests/test_docs.py` is expected to fail on the gap until the two meet.)

**Why a pre-screen.** `mimo-v2.6:9b` failed the GPU gate (87%, D-97), so the comparison had one local candidate left. Five small Ollama models were tried on five bits each (prompts 60 to 64, outside the run's 60) with `sim/screen_local.py`: the harness's own warm-up client, `measure_placement` and `generate_bit` at the production timing (10 s per attempt, 12 s over all, 2 retries), each model unloaded after its arm. Two arms: **`json_object`** (the local client's request so far: any JSON) and **`json_schema`** (the reply schema, `generate.REPLY_SCHEMA`, as `response_format: {"type": "json_schema", ...}`). **The schema matters for fairness:** Claude's replies are schema-enforced (`output_config.format`), and the local client only asked for any JSON, so the comparison would charge our client's weakness to the local models. Thinking is off for all five (`reasoning_effort: none`; verified for qwen3.5: 0 reasoning characters), so a timeout is not hidden thinking.

**Results, valid bits of 5 / p50 / max seconds over all five attempts (a timeout counts at its wall time). All five fit 100% on the GPU at context 8192 in both arms.**

| Model | json_object (coordinator) | json_schema (coordinator) | json_object (rerun) | json_schema (rerun) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bonsai-2:4b | 5/5, 5.21, 5.55 | 4/5, 4.55, 6.06 | 4/5, 4.96, 6.14 | 4/5, 5.33, 5.88 |
| qwen3.5:4b-q4_K_M | 3/5, 8.11, 12.03 | 4/5, 7.90, 12.03 | 5/5, 7.69, 9.43 | 4/5, 7.44, 8.82 |
| ministral-3:3b | 1/5, 1.89, 12.03 | 5/5, 8.81, 9.03 | 2/5, 9.21, 12.18 | 1/5, 6.45, 12.03 |
| gemma3:4b | 3/5, 5.65, 12.02 | 4/5, 5.64, 6.19 | 3/5, 11.20, 12.02 | 3/5, 5.50, 12.04 |
| qwen3:4b-instruct | 5/5, 6.31, 8.32 | 5/5, 7.36, 8.19 | 5/5, 7.82, 9.45 | 5/5, 6.17, 7.03 |

The first two numeric columns are the first run (by the coordinator, one afternoon); the last two are the rerun recorded in `reports/metrics/local_screen_json_object.json` and `local_screen_json_schema.json`, which replace the first run as the record. Failure classes in the rerun (`GenerationError` is a reply of the wrong shape, `LLMError` a timeout or transport failure): bonsai 1 `GenerationError` in each arm; qwen3.5 1 `GenerationError` with the schema; ministral 2 `GenerationError` and 1 `LLMError` without, 3 and 1 with; gemma3 2 `LLMError` without, 1 and 1 with; qwen3:4b-instruct none.

**What the two runs say, plainly.** The numbers move a lot between the runs for the same model and arm (ministral 5/5 then 1/5 with the schema, qwen3.5 3/5 then 5/5 without it). **At five bits the screen cannot show that the schema helps:** 5 of 5 has a Wilson 95% interval of about 57 to 100%, and the two runs disagree about the effect. What both runs agree on: **`qwen3:4b-instruct` had no failure in either arm in either run** (20 of 20 valid bits, p50 about 6 to 8 s, max under 9.5 s), and the other four each failed at least one bit in at least one of the four arm-runs. The schema is kept for parity with Claude (a fairness argument that holds without the screen), not because the screen proved it faster or safer. **The schema fixes the JSON shape, not the lengths:** a reply with 2 threads, or a thread of 4 lines, still validates against it and is still counted as the model's failure.

**Decisions.** (1) **Local candidates send the reply schema** (`llm.Client(json_schema=True)`, set by `make_client` and `make_warmup_client`; a dict `json_reply` becomes `response_format: json_schema` named "reply", else `json_object` as before). Gemini's client and every other caller keep `json_object`. The mode (`LOCAL_JSON_MODE`) is hashed into the generation digest, so no run folder mixes modes. (2) **`mimo-v2.6:9b` is removed; `qwen3:4b-instruct` replaces it** (Alibaba; `QWEN_OLLAMA_TAG`, $0; eligible like bonsai, subject to the same GPU gate). `gemma4:12b` stays the reference; the ballot stays four options (D-97).

**Options.** Keep mimo as a reference: it would be a second reference that adds a fourth reading per prompt for no decision, so no; `gemma4:12b` already shows the distance to a stronger model. `ministral-3:3b`: clean (5/5) in the coordinator's schema arm and 1/5 and 2/5 elsewhere, so not stable. `qwen3.5:4b-q4_K_M`: timeouts in the first run, a shape error in the rerun. `gemma3:4b`: a failed bit (a timeout or a wrong shape) in every arm-run. **qwen3:4b-instruct over ministral:** clean under both settings in both runs and the most headroom among the models that were ever 5/5 (max 9.5 s of the 12 s deadline): **a judgment call on n=5, not measured; the 60-prompt run decides.** Schema off: simplest, and the same request as before, but it keeps the unfair advantage of Claude's enforced shape. Schema on: parity, at the price of one more difference to explain in the report (the local models are constrained decoding, which also changes their speed and possibly their style: not measured).

**Not measured, said plainly.** Everything above is five bits per cell; the run's 60 prompts are what count. The schema's effect on speed and on the jokes' quality (constrained decoding can change both) is unmeasured; the funniness comparison is the user's blind vote. The reply schema allows any number of threads and lines, so a model that returns the wrong lengths is still a failure of the model. The screen used the default Ollama context (8192) and a laptop that was otherwise idle; the run shares the laptop with the user's other work.

**Power on the shipped ballot (computed in review, `simulate_decision_power(eligible=3, references=1)`, the real `decide()`, 400 runs).** Three models can win (Haiku, bonsai, qwen), so the best has to beat two rivals, as in D-94; the D-97 figures for two eligible no longer apply. Chance the truly funnier model is called clearly funnier, by its lead in latent sd: at 30 voted prompts 20% at 0.5, 57% at 0.75, 84% at 1.0, 99% at 1.5; at 60 (with the judge, an upper bound) 49%, 88%, 99%, 100%. A false winner with no real gap: about 1%. So the vote finds only a clear gap; a small one ends inconclusive and the cost tie-break (local is $0) decides.

## <a id="d-102"></a>D-102 · 2026-11-25 — DPO's judge is the D-95 judge, taught on 60 same-model pairs and gated on 60 more (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, with the Dec 8 demo still speaking the trained model. Tingyu's judge (#37, Exp 17) has been overdue since Nov 8, and DPO needs a pairwise judge.

**Choice.** The judge calibrated on the user's Exp 24 votes (D-95) also labels DPO's preference pairs. It must pass D-95's own bars (top-pick agreement, pairwise agreement, no model bias), and then the gate below.

**The risk.** The Exp 24 ballot compares bits from different models, which can differ a lot. DPO pairs are two bits from the same model, usually much closer in quality. A judge that matches the user on wide gaps can be near chance on narrow ones, and DPO would then learn the judge's quirks, not the user's taste.

**Pairs, fixed before any data.** The user ranks 120 pairs of two bits from the training base model (the Exp 24 local winner), on prompts outside the Exp 24 ballot and outside DPO's training prompts. Each pair is shown in both orders, so position bias cancels.
- **Teach set, 60 pairs:** given to the judge as examples of the user's taste, on top of the 30 Exp 24 votes.
- **Test set, 60 pairs:** never shown to the judge; only these decide the gate.

**Gate.** The lower end of the judge's 95% Wilson interval on the test set must clear 50%, which needs at least 38 of 60 (63%). The user moved from 20 pairs at a 60% point estimate after seeing its power, before any data.

**Power (computed, exact binomial).**
| Test pairs | Must agree on | Passes if the judge is truly 65% / 70% / 75% right | Passes by luck at 50% |
|---|---|---|---|
| 20 | 15 | 25% / 42% / 62% | 2% |
| 40 | 27 | 44% / 70% / 90% | 2% |
| **60** | **38** | **66% / 90% / 98%** | **3%** |
| 100 | 60 | 87% / 99% / 100% | 3% |
At 20 pairs a judge that is truly 70% right fails more often than it passes; at 60 it passes 9 times in 10.

**If the judge fails:** DPO does not run on it. The options are more of the user's votes, or Tingyu's judge.

**Options rejected.** (a) Wait for Tingyu's judge: overdue since Nov 8, and 13 days remain. (b) Adopt the D-95 judge on its own bars: tests only cross-model pairs, the wrong distribution for DPO. (c) 20 test pairs at a 60% point estimate: can't tell a good judge from chance (table). (d) The user's votes alone as DPO labels: too few pairs for DPO in the time left.

**Judgment calls, not measured.** The 60/60 split: more teaching pairs probably help the judge, but nothing here measures how much. The user's time: about 35 s a pair, so about 70 minutes for 120, in several sittings. Whether the frozen judge stays aligned as DPO moves the model away from its starting outputs (reward hacking): no check is planned during training.

## <a id="d-103"></a>D-103 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 24: bonsai's first block is archived, not scored, after a local server fault; its block is rerun (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, during the first Exp 24 run, before any vote or score.

**What happened.** qwen3:4b-instruct finished its block clean (60 of 60, 4.8 to 8.5 s). bonsai-2:4b then had 3 failures of its own (p06, p07, p09: wrong number of jokes, "expected 3 threads of 5"), and from p19 on **17 ReadTimeouts in a row** (p19 to p35, each at the 12 s limit). The Ollama tray app had been left running beside `ollama serve`; from about 14:25 it restarted a server every 1.2 s (`app.log`: "ollama exited", `server.log`: "bind: Only one usage of each socket address"), and `/api/ps` showed nothing loaded and the GPU idle while the calls timed out. After the tray app shut down (14:27:31), the same model answered a direct call in 6 s, 100% on the GPU at context 8192.

**Options.** (a) Count every attempt, as D-94 says: bonsai is out, partly for a fault in our machine. (b, chosen) **Archive bonsai's 36 attempts and rerun its block on a clean server**, the D-96 precedent: an outage on our side is not data about the model. (c) Drop only the timeouts: moves the bar after seeing data, which D-94 rules out.

**Cost of (b).** bonsai's 3 shape failures are seen, and its rerun is not blind to them; the rerun counts only its own attempts. Everything else stands: qwen's block, the placement and warm-up records. The archive is `data/model_comparison/gen-71242a99/bonsai-2-4b.aborted-tray-respawn-2026-11-25/` (gitignored, kept). The harness reads only exact model folder names, so the archive is never scored.

**Prevention.** Quit the tray app before a run. Not built: a check in `run` that stops when two Ollama servers are running or nothing is loaded after a warm-up.

## <a id="d-104"></a>D-104 · 2026-11-25 — Exp 24: drop the gemma4:12b reference, which cannot finish a bit in 12 s (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-25, during the Exp 24 rerun, before any vote.

**What happened.** The three candidates finished: qwen3:4b-instruct 60 of 60 valid, bonsai-2:4b 59 of 60 (one wrong joke count), Claude Haiku 5.5 58 of 60 (one timeout, one wrong joke count); all three are within the failure bar. gemma4:12b then timed out on its first 7 bits, every one at the 12 s limit, and its placement read unknown (the 1 GB report seen before, D-97). A direct call: about 145 output tokens in 13 to 15 s, no thinking (0 reasoning tokens), so about 10 tokens/s, with part of the model on the CPU. A bit is about 500 tokens, so about 50 s.

**Options.** (a, chosen) **Drop the reference.** The ballot is back to 3 options (about 38 minutes to vote). The reference's job, showing how far the small models are from a stronger one, is already done by Haiku, which is also the model the trained one must catch. (b) Keep it with a longer time limit for it alone (it can never be picked): about 50 minutes more generation, 12 more minutes of voting, and the record would say it ran on other timing.

**Mechanics (#73).** `gemma4-12b` is removed from `MODELS`; the reference rule stays in the code and is tested on a stand-in. The candidate list is hashed into the run folder's name, so the folder moves from `gen-71242a99` to `gen-2a512264`. It is renamed, not regenerated: the three remaining models' bits were made with identical settings, and only the list changed. Gemma's 7 failed attempts are archived beside the archived bonsai block (D-103).

**Power.** Unchanged in substance: three models can win, as before. With no reference: 20% at a 0.5 sd lead, 83% at 1.0, 98% at 1.5, on 30 voted prompts (D-101's figures).
