At bit N's end the real `director.update` and `director.decide` run exactly as before. Hit (guess equals the mode picked, exploration picks included): the prepared plans are used and `_bridge` returns at once because the prep is done. Miss: the task is cancelled and the bit is prepared as today, with bridge and filler lines. Which guess: `learn` (taught the partial reward) against `now` (model as it is); the better one in "judge, gaps x1" at 5 shows ships. Measured 0.72 against 0.48, so `learn`.
- **Same show with the guess on or off.** `tests/test_predict_next.py` compares the whole log (decisions with scores and probabilities, candidates, picks, reactions, rewards), the director's matrices and its RNG state, with `predict=True` and `False` on the same seeds.
- **Voice.** The voice is serial. At the guess the current bit's remaining renders are started first, and the guessed bit's first render waits for them (`gate`), so the lines the room is about to hear are never queued behind it. A render that fails and falls to the bank is rendered lazily and is not gated: rare, judgment call.
- **Discard hygiene (rewritten after the Opus review of PR #83, which reproduced two ways a wrong guess changed the show).** A guess runs the prep early and may throw it away, so it may only run when every stateful part it touches can be kept apart: a part must say so (`generate_speculative`, `fork()` or `speculative_safe`), else nothing is guessed. The selector is always forked (deepcopy). `BankGenerator` has `fork()` (its used-slot and assigned-slot state): used as the writer and as the bank fallback inside a guessed prep, its fork is adopted on a hit and dropped on a miss, so a wrong guess no longer uses up a slot the real bit takes (this was the first blocker: predict on and off differed after a miss on the bank path). `ThreadGenerator` keeps a guessed bit in `_spec`, keyed (bit, mode), apart from `_bits`, so it never answers the real request. A thrown-away guess is recorded in `_dead`, so a writer call that ends late (a worker thread cannot be cancelled) writes nothing.
- **Adopt only after the prep has ended (second blocker).** `adopt` and `played_thread` run after `await prep`, beside the log flush. Done right after the decision they ran while the bit was still being written: `adopt` was a no-op and the guessed bit landed in `_spec` later, and the premise was never told (a missing premise breaks D-110's no-repeat). The whole bit's guessed generation is one fetch under one key, so adopting cannot split its jokes across two fetches. Tested with the real `ThreadGenerator` machinery and a slow model call, hit and miss.
- **A fork that went stale is not adopted.** The show keeps a snapshot (pickle) of the real selector and of the parts that fork, taken when the forks are made, and compares it at the decision; if the original was used meanwhile (a bank consult after a failed voice in jokes 4 and 5) the guess is treated as a miss, `adopted: false` in its row.
- **Told premises.** The current bit's own prep has already told its premise before jokes 0 to 2 play, so a guessed prep sees the right `_told`.
- **Log (invariant 6).** A guessed prep's rows are held and written only on a hit, marked `prep.speculative: true`; a miss leaves no `prep` row. Every guess writes a `prediction` row right after the bit's `bit_decision`: `predicted`, `decided`, `hit`, `started_at`, and optional `after_joke`, `learned` (the copy was taught the partial reward, 3 labels), `adopted` (a right guess whose fork was stale is not used), `spec_stage` (`generating`, `safety`, `rendering` or `ready`: how far the prep had got) and `spec_prefetched` (a safety request went out), so the wasted work of misses can be counted. Both are optional additions to schema v2; replay ignores them and needed no change. `candidates` rows are written when a joke plays, so only the bit actually played has them. Abort cancels the guessed task; a held row never reaches the log.
- **Setting.** `PREDICT_NEXT_BIT` (default on, `0` turns it off; `run_show(predict=...)`).
- **Exp 29.** `stage_timing` marks speculative bits and leaves them out of the stage shares (their stage seconds ran during the previous bit); the wait and bridge columns keep them: that is the live before/after measure.

**Number that decides (Exp 31, `sim/evaluate_early.py`, `sim/evaluate_predict.py`).** Both bars were fixed in the module docstrings before running. Ten rooms: `base` gaps x0.5, x1, x2 under judge and human taste, `noise` x0.5 and x2, `fatigue` 0 and 0.3; 5-show horizon, 1,000 paired seeds; the 30-show rows are reported and do not decide.
- **(a) Deciding at joke 3** passes if the far end of the 95% CI of (early minus end) laughs per show is at least -1.0 in every room. **Missed in 8 of 10 rooms** (about -1.3 per show, up to -2.1 with low noise or high fatigue). After joke 5, still one update behind, it costs as much, so the cost is the delay. Not shipped. The bar was not loosened after the result: under -0.02 reward per bit (2.4 laughs per show) every room would pass; that is the user's call and was not taken.
- **(b) Guessing** passes if the 95% CI of the hit rate has its lower end at least 0.5 in every room. **Met in all 10 rooms**: lower ends 0.69 to 0.74, hit rate about 0.71 to 0.75 against a ceiling of about 0.83 (exploring picks a random mode). Reward is identical by construction and by the golden test, so it is not re-measured.
- **Estimate, not measured live:** seconds saved per show = hit rate x about 10 s of prep x 7 gaps, about 50 s.
- **Cost of a miss:** one local writer call (free) and, when the prep got to the safety stage (`spec_prefetched`), one Haiku safety prefetch (cents), on about 3 in 10 of the 7 gaps. **A miss can be slower than no guess:** the cancelled prep's writer calls (up to 3, one per thread) cannot be cancelled in their worker threads and may keep Ollama (`NUM_PARALLEL=3`) busy ahead of the real bit's calls. Not measured; the live check below shows it as a longer wait on missed bits.
- **Live hit rate may be lower than the sim's.** The sim always has 3 labels at joke 3. A bit with fewer falls back to the model as it is, which hit 0.48 in the sim. `python -m sim.stage_timing` now reports the live hit rate from the `prediction` rows (all, and the ones made on 3 labels), the number used, and the safety requests wasted.
Depends on the made-up `audience.yaml` values `noise`, `fatigue` and the `base` gaps (all varied), and on eps = 0.2, a uniform prior and 5 shows. The user runs the live check: `python -m sim.stage_timing <ids>` against shows `6c09c721b0bb` and `0e102d77d795`.

**Judgment calls, not measured.** That guessing after joke 3 (not 2 or 4) is the right point; that a wrong guess's waste is tolerable; that not gating a fall-through render is fine; the 0.5 hit-rate bar; 1.0 laugh per show as the tolerance for (a) (Exp 18's bar).

**Live check (2026-11-26, `sim/evaluate_gaps.py`, `reports/metrics/live_gaps.json`).** Show selection: the 2 shows named in D-115's plan plus every finished show on c474e51 (no guess) or c188c88 (guess), all sim room, gen-v4 bonsai-2:4b. Before: `6c09c721b0bb`, `0e102d77d795`, `c514a01f04f7`, `9fedcfa07c3f`. After: `5ea47db7b248`, `a7290b75cf24`, `2ab2d1c2c682`, `94aef761a5e6` (the fourth was added to balance the sets). `6c09c721b0bb` is from before D-112, so its bridge lines are cached lines, not filler, and its count includes the bit-0 one; `e204ec80e62e` (gen-v4, #78, before the #79 schema) is left out as a different code version. **Different shows, not paired seeds; 4 against 4; the made-up sim audience reacted.** Waits start at `bit_end` (0.3 to 2.5 s after the last joke's audio in both sets); they depend on `audience.yaml` only through when reactions arrive, the stage times do not. Bridge lines are all `bridge` rows.
- **Hit rate against Exp 31(b)'s bar (95% CI lower end at least 0.5): 23 of 28 right (0.82), Wilson 95% interval 0.64 to 0.92, lower end 0.64, above the bar.** The interval is optimistic: guesses within a show are not independent. All 28 were made on 3 labels, 23 used, 5 safety requests wasted (1, 1, 2, 1 per show). Exp 31's sim rate was about 0.7 (ceiling 0.83).
- **Wait before the first joke, bits 1 and up:** before p50 13.7 s, p95 26.7 s over 28 gaps, 0 zero-wait gaps; after p50 0.0 s, p95 18.3 s over 28 gaps, 21 zero-wait gaps (at most 0.1 s). The 7 gaps that still waited took 6.1 to 18.7 s: 5 follow a wrong guess (12.1 to 18.7 s, the whole prep back in the wait) and 2 a right guess whose prep was not ready (6.1 and 6.2 s). The p95 stays high because a wrong guess waits as long as before; a miss being slower than no guess is not visible (those 5 waits sit inside the before range of 9.8 to 27.4 s).
- **The number D-115 estimated (total wait over bits 1+, per show):** before 108.7 s (88.6, 99.8, 123.9, 122.6), after 21.6 s (24.5, 12.7, 37.1, 12.1): about 87 s saved per show against the ~50 s estimate (hit rate x 10 s x 7 gaps). Different shows, so an indication, not a paired measure.
- **Bridge lines per show:** before 16, 16, 19, 19 (mean 17.5); after 4, 2, 6, 2 (mean 3.5).
- **Ratings beside, not a claim:** before 2, 1, 2 (`9fedcfa07c3f` has none); after 3, 3, 2, 2.
On the waits in these 4 shows D-115 did what it set out to do; n is small. No new decision.

## <a id="d-116"></a>D-116 · 2026-11-26 — Keep a show ledger: one row per logged show, with the user's rating as the outcome, and commit the played lines (Exp 32) (user)
The user asked for the joke quality of the show runs to be tracked for the report, and later (same day) for the bits themselves to be kept for comparison, then to push them. Light log: reporting infrastructure, not a model choice.

**What is tracked (`sim/show_ledger.py`, `reports/metrics/show_ledger.json`).** Per show: session id, start time (UTC), room, status, bits played, code version (`git_sha`) and the pull request that first merged it into `main` (first-parent, `Merge pull request #N`; the sha when git cannot say), writer (generator id: prompt and model), guess on/off (`prediction` rows present), bridge rule (from the `cache` row: none, repeats allowed, skips heard lines), the user's rating, delivery signals (bank share, lost threads by error class, median and worst wait and total wait before bits' first jokes, bridge lines, guess hit rate) and a hash per bit. Setups = writer x guess x bridge rule, ordered by first use. Wait logic is `sim/evaluate_gaps.show_report`, bank share `sim/acceptance.bank_jokes`; nothing duplicated.

**Why the rating is the outcome and sim reactions are not.** Invariant 5: the episode rating is the reported outcome. The rating is the user's own in every room. In a `sim` room the reactions and bit rewards are the made-up audience's (`audience.yaml`), so they say nothing about whether the jokes were funny; they are recorded per show and per setup in a block labelled "simulated audience", apart from the "live" block, and no pooled number exists (a test fails if one appears; mutation-checked). Only one live show finished (`43251e36e02d`), so live reaction evidence is n = 1.

| Option for the bits | For | Against | Choice |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) Ledger with ids and hashes only; no text anywhere in git | Matches "no session data" the safest way | The report cannot show which bit was good; the user cannot compare setups side by side | Rejected by the user's later call |
| (b) Played lines in local files under `data/` (gitignored) | Nothing leaves the laptop | The comparison cannot be cited or reviewed; lost with the laptop | Offered first, replaced by (c) |
| (c) Commit the played lines under `reports/show_bits/<setup>/<session>.md` and an index | The lines are generated (writer or bank), safety-filtered and played, so they are the evidence the report cites; reviewable in the PR | Text enters git; must never include anything people said | **Chosen (user: "These jokes are generated, not anyone's content. We can push it.")** |

**What is committed now:** only `joke_played.text` and `bridge.text` (generated, bank or earlier generated lines, safety-filtered, played) with each line's reaction level and its room label ("live" or "simulated audience"), the show's setup and the user's rating. The ledger JSON still holds only ids, numbers and a 16-hex hash per bit (sha256 of the played lines), so the report can cite a bit by hash. **Still not committed:** session logs, the recordings, ballots, reaction keys as rows, rJokes, transcript text, anything said by people present. **What was checked for live shows:** the log schema (`backend/app/log.py` `EVENTS`) has no field for transcribed or audio-derived text: a live show's rows hold generated text, button keys and levels, the venue/energy/age tags and the rating; speech exists only in the WAV beside the log, which is never read or committed. So live bits are included. The export reads only the two text fields above (a test with a planted string in every other field checks it). The PR template's "no session data" box still holds: only derived generated lines are exported, never the log.

**Layout of the show files.** Each file reads top to bottom as the room heard it: header (date, setup in plain words, simulated or live, rating), then every line in audio order, with the opener, filler and replayed lines labelled, bank lines marked, a plain reaction word after each line (for a sim room, labelled "simulated audience"), and a one-line gap note between bits. `reports/show_bits/index.md` lists shows by setup, newest first.

**How to update after each show:** `python -m sim.show_ledger --record` (the cache key is the logs' names, sizes and mtimes plus the head of `main`, so a new show re-records), `python -m sim.show_ledger export` to refresh `reports/show_bits/`, then `python scripts/run_notebook.py notebooks/decision-plot.ipynb`.

**Review fixes (Opus, same day).** The pull request of a show is its own merge when its `git_sha` is on `main`'s first-parent line (every logged sha is), else the first merge that brought it in (before this, every show was credited to the next PR). gen-v4 shows on #78 code (before D-111's counted schema and re-ask, which landed in #79) are a separate setup, "gen-v4 before D-111 fixes", because `generator_id` did not change when the behaviour did. A show cut short before its first guess on guess code (#83 on) is filed with the guess (`prediction_inferred`); the log has no flag for `PREDICT_NEXT_BIT`. The permutation test cannot reach p below 0.05 at 1 to 4 ratings a side (2 against 2 at best 0.33, 2 against 4 0.13, 3 against 4 0.057), so `within_noise` is null ("too few ratings to tell") unless the sizes could have rejected; the best possible p is stored beside each p (`p_min`). Gap notes count replayed (`cached`) lines apart from filler; a bridge row with no `kind` is filler. Dates are the machine's local time with the UTC offset stored. `export` clears the old files first. The headline wait is the ledger's own pair of setups (gen-v4 with no guess and no heard lines, then with the guess); D-115's live check used a different "before" set (it included `6c09c721b0bb`, which the ledger files one setup earlier), hence 108.7 s to 21.6 s there. Show `e9a42ccc6b36` logs gen-v3 on code that already had gen-v4 (the backend was not restarted after a merge); it is aborted and counts nowhere.

**Judgment calls, not measured:** the setup axes; the 0.05 line for the permutation test and requiring 2 ratings per side; the label for each setup change on the chart (`CHANGES`); the pull-request mapping by first merge; excluding bank-only shows from the chart (no model wrote them); counting aborted shows as rows only. Ratings are the user's, one rater, 1 to 4 per setup, so differences are within noise and the report says so.

## <a id="d-117"></a>D-117 · 2026-11-26 — Give each bit a topic and two robot facts so the writer stops landing on the robot's hardware (Exp 33) (user)
The user chose the problem, the options (character sheet plus topic list now, tonight's context later) and the bars; the lead agent specified it and a Sonnet agent built it. Result: keep off. Repetition bar passed, funniness bar failed (table at the end of this entry).

**The problem.** In the 35 logged shows, 249 of 882 played jokes (28%) name robot hardware (battery, power, data, charging, processor top). The personality is also bland. Cause, from `ThreadGenerator._fetch`: the writer's only inputs are a one-sentence identity, the mode, venue and energy, and the premises told this show. The told list resets each show, so nothing stops the next show from writing the same bit; and the identity has no specifics to draw on, so the model reaches for its own hardware.

| Option | For | Against | Choice |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Topic list: one everyday topic per bit, never reused across shows on this laptop | Directly breaks the repeat; cheap; no new inputs at show time | A topic alone does not give the robot a voice | Chosen now (user) |
| B. Operator-typed "tonight" context | Fresh, relevant material | Needs a UI and an operator who types; a later PR | Later PR, not this one. Scope now (user, 2026-11-26): the operator's typed facts about tonight, plus 5 to 10 of the day's headlines from a news RSS feed and the day's stock market move as a number. Both pass the safety check (tragedies and named people are dropped) and the operator approves them on the operator screen before the show |
| C. Character sheet: specific facts about the robot, 2 per bit, rotated | Gives bits something specific; punches at self (invariant 1) | A fact can pull the bit back to hardware if the model leans on it | Chosen now (user) |
| D. Topic seeds from rJokes | Large, varied | Copying risk, and the rJokes-text rule | Rejected |
| E. Train it away (SFT or DPO) | None at show time | Both sharpen what the model already writes; DPO narrows diversity; 60 + 60 pairs is too few. Judgment call, not measured | Rejected |

**Design (C + A).** `backend/app/material.py`, data in `backend/app/material/character-v1.yaml` (facts) and `topics-v1.yaml` (topics; `adult: true` marks the ones a teen room skips; `from: character` marks the robot's own life as a bit's whole topic). One `ShowMaterial` per show with a logged seed orders topics and facts.
- **Topic.** About 1 bit in 3 (`CHARACTER_EVERY = 3`: bits 1, 4, 7) takes the next unused `from: character` topic, the others the next unused everyday one; an empty pool falls back to the other before anything recycles. Unused means no earlier logged show (any room, sim or live; `used_topics` mirrors `cache.heard_texts`) and no earlier bit this show. When every topic is used, the least recently used of the bit's pool is taken and the row says `recycled: true`.
- **Facts.** The 2 least used this show, ties by the seeded order.
- **Used means played.** A topic or fact is spent when the bit's thread is played (`played_thread`), as the told premises are. A bit written on a guess (D-115) spends nothing when discarded; an adopted bit keeps the material it was written with; drawing twice for one bit gives one answer (a draw is a pure function of what has been played). Tested in `tests/test_material.py`.
- **Request.** With `MATERIAL=1` the user JSON gets `topic` and `robot_facts`, and one fixed paragraph is appended to the system prompt (the PROMPTS entries stay frozen; it works with any prompt id, the live one at run time). The generator id gets `+mat-v1`.
- **Log (invariant 6).** One `material` row per bit that plays: bit, topic, from, facts, sheet_id, list_id, seed, recycled. Schema stays v2; every reader dispatches on event names, and a test loads a log with these rows through the director, the cache and `load_sessions`.
- **Setting.** `MATERIAL`, off by default until Exp 33 passes.

**Exp 33 (`sim/material.py`, offline, the user runs it).** 5 consecutive simulated shows of 8 bits, 40 bits per arm, modes and rooms from one fixed seed and identical for both arms. A: the live prompt, material off. B: the same prompt, material on, topics skipping those used in earlier shows of the run (empty history at the start, so it does not depend on this laptop's logs). The played thread is thread 0. Writer: the live one (bonsai-2:4b on Ollama); bits are written one at a time.
- **Metric 1 (repetition, gates).** Share of bits whose premise and lines are about the robot's own hardware, labeled by a Claude Haiku 5.5 judge with one fixed yes/no question, blind to the arm; a word-list share beside it as a cross-check. **Bar (user): B at most 15% and the paired bootstrap 95% CI of (B - A), paired by bit slot, entirely below 0.** Reported with no bar: the share for character-topic bits against everyday-topic bits.
- **Metric 2 (user, blind, gates funniness).** 20 pairs (same slot, A against B, sides randomized by seed). For each: (a) which is funnier, (b) which sounds more like a specific character. **Bar (user) for (a): B wins at least 10 of 20; a tie is not a win.** (b) is reported with a Wilson 95% interval, not gated. 20 pairs detect only large differences: if B is truly preferred 60% of the time it reaches 10 wins about 87% of the time, and under a coin flip it still reaches 10 about 59% of the time. So the gate is "not clearly worse", and a pass is weak evidence of "funnier".
- **Adopt** (flip the `MATERIAL` default to on, in the same PR) only when both bars pass. A failed repetition bar cannot be rescued by the pairs.
- **What is made up.** No `audience.yaml` value is involved: there is no sim room reward. The slot modes and rooms are seeded draws; the judge's question and the 15% bar are judgment calls (15% is about half of the live 28%).
- **Commands (user).** `python -m sim.material run --env-file backend/.env`, then `python -m sim.material review`, then `python -m sim.material score --record`.

**Judgment calls, not measured.** 2 facts per bit (not 1 or 3); character topics for 1 bit in 3; the paragraph's wording; that a fact is rotated by least-used rather than random; the 15% bar.

**Result (2026-11-26, the user ran `run`, `review`, `score --record`; record `reports/metrics/material.json`). Verdict `keep_off`: `MATERIAL` stays off by default.** 5 shows of 8 bits, 40 slots per arm, all bits delivered in both arms, no thread lost, 0 recycled topics (40 distinct topics, 15 character-topic bits), judge failures 0, 20 of 20 pairs answered. A = gen-v5 (material off), B = gen-v5 + material.

| Check | Result | Bar | Met |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hardware share, B (material on) | 12% (5 of 40 bits) | at most 15% | yes |
| Hardware share, A (material off) | 88% (35 of 40) | beside the bar | |
| Paired B - A over 40 slots | -0.750, CI -0.900 to -0.600 | CI entirely below 0 | yes |
| Word-list share, A / B (cross-check) | 90% / 42% | beside the bar | |
| Hardware share, character-topic / everyday bits (B) | 20% (3 of 15) / 8% (2 of 25) | beside the bar | |
| (a) Funnier: B wins | 6 of 20 (A 9, ties 5) | at least 10 | no |
| (b) More like a specific character: B wins | 10 of 20 (A 7, ties 3), Wilson 95% 30% to 70% | beside the bar | |

Writer time per bit: A p50 4.3 s, p95 7.4 s; B p50 4.4 s, p95 7.6 s. Arm B had 1 bad reply (A had 0); it was counted, not dropped.

**Reading.** The repetition bar passed by a wide margin: the share of bits about the robot's own hardware fell from 88% to 12%, and the paired CI is far from 0. The funniness bar failed as defined: B needed 10 wins of 20 and got 6; the user preferred the material-off bit more often (A 9, B 6, 5 ties). With 20 pairs this does not show B is worse (6 against 9 is well inside coin-flip noise), only that the pre-set bar was not met, and under the rule a failed bar means keep off. Nothing was rescued by (b), which is reported and not gated: B was picked as more like a specific character in 10 of 20 (A 7, ties 3), an interval of 30% to 70%, so no direction. The word-list cross-check agrees on the direction (90% to 42%) but is far less extreme than the judge's 12%, so the word list flags many B bits the judge does not (probably battery or power words used in passing); the bar rides on the judge, which labels the bit's premise and lines. Character-topic bits stayed more hardware-heavy (20%) than everyday ones (8%) even with material on, on 15 and 25 bits.

**Outcome.** Keep `MATERIAL` off. Not run: a variant with fewer or softer facts, or everyday topics only (character-topic bits are where hardware survives). Whether to try one is the user's call. Judgment call, not measured: that the funniness loss comes from the facts rather than the topics (the run did not separate them).


## <a id="d-118"></a>D-118 · 2026-11-26 — Try the topic alone, with no robot facts, against Exp 33's old bits (Exp 34) (user)
The user's hypothesis after Exp 33 failed on funniness: the two robot facts make the bit stiff and pull it back toward hardware, so give the writer a topic only. Result: keep off. Repetition bar failed (topic only 17.5% hardware, bar 15%), so the funniness pairs were not judged (table at the end of this entry).

**This is the second attempt at the same bars after Exp 33 failed.** Under no real difference, one attempt reaches 10 wins of 20 about 59% of the time (D-117's figure, ties ignored), so two attempts reach it at least once about 83% of the time (1 - 0.41 squared). A pass here is weak evidence of "funnier", and weaker than the same pass would have been on a first try. 20 pairs kept (user choice, 2026-11-26, over 30); 20 pairs only detect large differences.

| Option | For | Against | Choice |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Topic only, no facts (arm C) | Tests the user's hypothesis directly; the topics are the part that fixed the repeat; cheapest change | If the facts were what made the bit specific, "more like a character" falls | Chosen now (user) |
| B. Fewer facts (1) or softer facts | Keeps some voice | Two things changed from D-117 at once if the wording also moves; does not isolate the cause | Not run |
| C. Everyday topics only (drop robot-life topics) | Robot-life topics keep the most hardware (20% in Exp 33) | Loses the robot's own life as a subject; also confounded with the facts | Not run |
| D. Leave it off | No more risk of a lucky pass | The repeat problem stays (28% of live jokes on hardware) | Not chosen |

**Design.** `MATERIAL` selects the variant: `1` (or `true`, `yes`, `on`, `full`) is D-117 unchanged, `topic` is the new one, anything else is off (still the default). `ShowMaterial(variant=...)` draws the same topics as before; in the topic variant `facts` is empty and `sheet_id` is null.
- **Request.** User JSON has `venue`, `energy`, `premises_already_told` and `topic`, with no `robot_facts` key. The system prompt gets `TOPIC_PARAGRAPH`, a separate fixed paragraph that mentions only the topic and keeps the "not about your own hardware" sentence. D-117's `MATERIAL_PARAGRAPH` is byte-identical (a test holds a copy). Generator id suffix `+mat-topic-v1` (D-117's stays `+mat-v1`).
- **Same as D-117.** Topic list, character topics 1 bit in 3 (`CHARACTER_EVERY = 3`), "used means played", the draw order for one seed (facts are still shuffled, never drawn, so the same seed gives the same topic order).
- **Log row.** The `material` row gets an optional `variant` (`full` or `topic`; a D-117 row has none and reads as `full`), with `facts: []` for topic. Schema stays v2.

**Exp 34 (`sim/material.py --exp 34`, offline, the user runs it).** Arm C = topic only. Same 40 slots (5 shows x 8 bits), same modes and rooms, same writer (bonsai-2:4b on Ollama), and the same material seeds as Exp 33, so C's topics are exactly B's topics from Exp 33: C and B differ only by the facts.
- **Arm A is not rewritten.** Exp 33's 40 material-off bits are read from `data/material/run-94cc8faa/bits/*-A.json` (gitignored; that folder name is Exp 33's digest and still matches the code). The run refuses to start if the folder is missing (a code change moved its digest), a bit is missing, or a stored bit does not match its slot (slot id, arm, mode, no topic). Arm A's judge verdicts are reused from Exp 33's judge cache too, so A is labeled once.
- **Metric 1 (gates).** Hardware share by the same Claude Haiku judge with the same yes/no question, blind to the arm; word-list share beside it. **Bar: C at most 15% AND the paired bootstrap 95% CI of (C - A), paired by slot, entirely below 0.**
- **Metric 2 (gates).** 20 new blind pairs, C against A, same slot, sides randomized by pair seed 34 (Exp 33 used 32), the same two questions. The pairs come only from the 20 slots Exp 33's review did not show (pre-merge review fix, before any run): the user has read the arm A bit of each of Exp 33's 20 pairs, and with seed 34 alone 12 of the 20 new pairs would have reused one, so a familiar bit could give away which side is A. `exp33_shown` recomputes Exp 33's pairs (plus any slot answered) and the run refuses when Exp 33's arm B bits are gone. With all 40 C bits written, the draw is exactly the other 20 slots; a C bit with no thread there makes the review short, not refilled. **Bar for (a) funnier: C wins at least 10 of 20; a tie is not a win.** (b) is reported with an interval and not gated. Pairs are stored under `data/material/run-<digest>/review.json` (gitignored), as in Exp 33.
- **Adopt** (make `MATERIAL` default to `topic`, in the same PR) only when both bars pass; that flip happens after the user runs Exp 34, not before.
- **Record** `reports/metrics/material_topic.json` via `sim.cache.run_or_load`, seed string `exp34` (the slots keep Exp 33's seed so A matches).
- **Commands (user).** `python -m sim.material run --exp 34 --env-file backend/.env`, then `python -m sim.material review --exp 34`, then `python -m sim.material score --exp 34 --record`.

**What is made up.** No `audience.yaml` value is involved (no sim room reward). Slot modes and rooms are seeded draws; the judge's question and the 15% bar are judgment calls carried over from D-117.

**Judgment calls, not measured.** The wording of `TOPIC_PARAGRAPH`; keeping the hardware sentence in it; that the 20 unseen slots are a fair sample of the 40 (they are Exp 33's seeded leftovers, not chosen by content); that the facts (not the topics) are what cost the laughs (the hypothesis this run tests, with only 20 pairs).

**Result (2026-11-26, the user ran `run --exp 34`; record `reports/metrics/material_topic.json`). Verdict `keep_off`: `MATERIAL` stays off by default.** 5 shows of 8 bits, 40 slots per arm, all bits delivered in both arms, 0 recycled topics (40 distinct, 15 character-topic bits), judge failures 0, writer on the GPU after the run. A = gen-v5 (material off, Exp 33's stored bits), C = gen-v5 + topic.

| Check | Result | Bar | Met |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hardware share, C (topic only) | 17.5% (7 of 40 bits) | at most 15% | **no** |
| Hardware share, A (material off) | 87.5% (35 of 40) | beside the bar | |
| Paired C - A over 40 slots | -0.700, CI -0.850 to -0.525 | CI entirely below 0 | yes |
| Word-list share, A / C (cross-check) | 90% / 55% | beside the bar | |
| Hardware share, character-topic / everyday bits (C) | 40% (6 of 15) / 4% (1 of 25) | beside the bar | |
| Same split in Exp 33's B (topic + facts) | 20% (3 of 15) / 8% (2 of 25) | for comparison | |
| (a) Funnier: C wins | not judged (0 of 20 answered) | at least 10 | no (by rule) |
| (b) More like a specific character | not judged | beside the bar | |

Arm C: 1 thread lost, 0 bad replies (A: 0, 0). Writer time per bit: A p50 4.3 s, p95 7.4 s; C p50 4.8 s, p95 7.3 s.

**Reading.** Taking the facts away did not lower hardware; it raised it on the robot's-own-life topics (20% with facts, 40% without), where the facts were giving the bit something else to talk about. Everyday topics are near hardware-free with or without facts (8% and 4%, 25 bits). Exp 33's B and Exp 34's C differ only by the facts (same slots, modes, topics), so the gap on robot-life topics is the facts' effect on 15 bits, a small sample.

**Pairs not judged, by rule.** The pre-set rule says a failed repetition bar cannot be rescued by the pairs, so the user was told and agreed (2026-11-26) not to judge the 20 pairs. The funniness bar is therefore "not met" by default, not by a vote.

**Outcome.** Keep `MATERIAL` off. Not adopted, not run: everyday topics only (4% here looks promising), because that idea was picked after seeing this data and would be a third attempt at the same bar, with the multiple-attempts penalty on the funniness gate. Whether to try it is the user's call.
## <a id="d-119"></a>D-119 · 2026-11-26 — Give the writer tonight's context: approved headline gists, the market line and one optional typed line (Exp 35) (user)
The user chose the problem, the scope of option B and every option below (2026-11-26); typed facts were then cut to at most 1 by the user. The two bars of Exp 35 were **set by Claude, confirmed by the user 2026-11-26**. The lead agent specified it and a Sonnet agent built it. Results: Exp 35, run by the user 2026-11-26, below.

**Numbering note.** PR #86 (show ledger) holds D-116 and Exp 32; `feat/material` holds D-117 (Exp 33, material kept off after it) and D-118 (Exp 34, topic alone), so this entry is D-119 and Exp 35 (first written as D-117 / Exp 33, renumbered 2026-11-26). `tests/test_docs.py` requires contiguous IDs; D-116 to D-118 are now on `main` (#86, #87) and merged in.

**The problem.** Generated bits kept landing on the robot's hardware (D-117 measured 28% of played jokes). D-117 gives the writer a topic list and a character sheet; option B of its table is material from the day itself: fresher than any list, and about tonight, which a fixed list cannot be.

| Decision | Options | For / against | Choice |
|---|---|---|---|
| Where a headline goes | slot 1 bit in 4 (bits 2 and 6) as the topic / background in every bit / none | A topic gets a whole bit built on it; as background alone a small model mostly ignores it. A headline in every bit would make 8 bits about the news and collide with D-117's character bits (1, 4, 7) | Bits where `bit % 4 == 2` take the next unused approved gist as `topic`; every bit also gets the market line and the fact as `tonight` background (user) |
| Names | generic ("a GPU company", "a tech CEO", "a senator") / keep names / drop headlines that have names | Keeping names breaks the no-real-or-named-people rule and risks a joke at a real person's expense; dropping named headlines leaves almost none (nearly every headline has a name) | Generic names for both companies and people; the rule is unchanged (user). **Amended by the user the same day:** holidays, festivals, important dates and sports teams or events may stay capitalized, because a headline about the Super Bowl or a team is not about a private person; companies, products, people and places stay generic |
| Off-limits categories | none / death, disaster, crime, war / also politics and health | A robot that jokes about a tragedy is the worst case of the show. Politics and health are what a room talks about, and still go through the safety check | Death, disaster, crime and war are dropped before the operator sees them; politics and health stay (user) |
| Sources | Google News top-stories RSS + Stooq's S&P 500 daily CSV / Google News + FRED's SP500 CSV / BBC | All free and keyless; Google News gives US-flavoured top stories in one feed. Stooq now blocks scripts (below) | Google News RSS and FRED (user, after Stooq failed; first choice was Stooq) |
| Typed facts | up to 3 lines / at most 1 line / none | Headlines and the market already add content; a typing operator is a cost at show time | **At most 1 line, optional, empty by default (`MAX_TONIGHT_FACTS = 1`), one single-line input (user, after the first spec said up to 3)** |

**Design.** `backend/app/tonight.py`. Claude Haiku calls (the safety-check model and client, `llm.from_env(role="safety_check")`) turn the raw headlines (10 in Exp 35; up to 40 since, 15 per call) into one-sentence gists with a category (`none`, `death`, `disaster`, `crime`, `war`) as JSON; the reply must answer every id once or the headline half fails. Then, per gist: dropped if the category is not `none`; a cheap name check (`name_check`: a capitalized word that does not start a sentence and is not in a small allowlist like `S&P`, `AI`, `US`, or a first name from `safety.FIRST_NAMES`; acronyms count unless allowlisted) counted separately; then `safety.prefetch` and `check` at the strictest age (`teens`, because the room is unknown at approval time). Counts are kept: fetched, dropped by category, no gist, rejected by name check, rejected by safety, kept. The typed line goes through the same safety check. The market is the last two numeric closes of FRED's SP500 CSV as one templated sentence with a rounded percent and the date, no model involved.
- **Fetch and approval.** `GET /tonight` (off unless `TONIGHT` is on) fetches once a day and caches to gitignored `data/tonight/<date>.json` (a day where both halves failed is not cached; `?refresh=true` refetches). The operator UI shows the market line (ticked by default), one checkbox per gist (all unticked) and one single-line fact input with a Check button (`POST /tonight/check`). The `start` message carries `tonight: {facts, market, gists}`; the server checks it again (`make_context`): a gist or market line the day's fetch did not produce is dropped (the browser is not trusted), and the typed line and every gist go through the safety check again (fails closed: no key means no approved text).
- **Request.** With approved context the user JSON gets `tonight: {facts, market}` for every bit and, for bits 2 and 6, `topic`: the next unused approved gist. One fixed paragraph is appended to the system prompt (`PROMPTS` stay frozen, like D-117's paragraph); the generator id gets `+tonight-v1`. The integration in `generate.py._fetch` is a few lines (`bit` is passed in, the fields are added), isolated because `feat/material` edits the same function.
- **Used means played.** A headline is spent in `ThreadGenerator.played_thread`. A draw is a pure function of what has been played, so a D-115 guess that is discarded spends nothing, an adopted one spends when its thread is told, and a bit that fell back to the bank leaves its headline for the next headline bit. Tested.
- **Log (invariant 6).** A `tonight` row right after `show_start` (facts, market, approved gists, and in `report` the counts fetched, dropped by category, rejected by name check, rejected by safety, kept, approved, rejected at start, source URLs, fetch time) and a `tonight_use` row for each played headline bit. Schema stays v2; a test loads a log with both rows through the director, the cache and `load_sessions`.
- **Network.** Plain `User-Agent: humor-me-tonight/1.0`, no cookies, nothing about the user.
- **If a fetch fails or nothing is approved** the show runs exactly as today (tested at the generator, the builder and the payload).
- **Merge note.** `feat/material` also writes `topic` in `_fetch`, for every bit. When both merge, the tonight lines must come after material's so tonight's headline wins for bits 2 and 6; material would then draw an everyday topic it does not use for those bits (it spends it when played). Decide then whether a bit with a headline should skip material's draw.

**Found at build time: Stooq no longer answers scripts; the user chose FRED.** One fetch of `https://stooq.com/q/d/l/?s=^spx&i=d` (2026-11-26, generic User-Agent) returned HTTP 200 with an HTML page that runs a JavaScript proof-of-work "browser check", not the CSV. Google News RSS answered normally. The user then picked `https://fred.stlouisfed.org/graph/fredgraph.csv?id=SP500` (checked by the user with this User-Agent: header `observation_date,SP500`, rows like `2026-11-24,7201.09`). FRED marks a day with no close as `.`: `parse_market` skips non-numeric rows and takes the last two numeric closes. FRED lags the market by a day or two, so the line names the date ("The S&P 500 fell 0.5% on Nov 24."). Anything that is not that CSV raises, the market half fails alone and the show runs without a market line. The parser is tested on a synthetic sample with a `.` row; the Stooq code is gone.

**Names the gist may keep (user, 2026-11-26).** The gist prompt now lets a holiday, festival, important date, sports team or sports event stay capitalized and returns a `keep` list (`{name, type: occasion|sports}`, each name must appear in the gist or it is dropped). Two tiers, both in the safety rules' capitals check (`safety._named_person`): (1) `safety.OCCASIONS`, a static list of major holidays, shopping days and events (Thanksgiving, Black Friday, the Super Bowl, the Olympics and so on); their words join `CAPS_OK`, so they are allowed everywhere, jokes and gists (side effect: words like `New`, `Day`, `Black`, `Super` are now allowed capitalized mid-sentence); (2) sports names are dynamic: only the `keep` names of the **approved** gists of this show are allowed, through an `allow_caps` parameter threaded through `_named_person`, `_check`, `check_rules`, `check` and `prefetch` (default empty, behaviour unchanged; the two-argument `_check` call is kept when it is empty). `LiveSafety.allow_caps` is set at show start from the `TonightContext` (empty when tonight is off), because the jokes of a headline bit repeat the team name and would otherwise be rejected to the bank. The first-name list and the title rule still apply to every token, so a team name containing a first name is rejected; the LLM verdict is unchanged. The day's check allows the kept words of all surviving gists; at show start `make_context` checks again with only the approved ones. The `tonight` log row carries `allowed_names`. Risk, judgment call, not measured: a team name that is also a common capitalized word, and a Haiku that lists something wrong under `keep` (the name check and safety still read it).

**Exp 35 (`sim/tonight.py`, the user runs it).** `fetch` runs the same pipeline as `GET /tonight`, then you answer for each kept gist "approve?" and "contains a name?" and may type 0 or 1 line. `run` writes 10 pairs (the approved gists cycled) in a seeded mode and room with the live writer (`writer_client()`, bonsai on Ollama, one call at a time): A is the live prompt with no tonight context, B is the live prompt plus tonight (the gist as the topic at bit 2, your line, the market line). `review` shows the pairs blind and seeded, asking (a) which is funnier and (b) which feels more like it is happening tonight. Checkpoints are under gitignored `data/tonight/exp35/`; the record is `reports/metrics/tonight.json`, saved by `score --record`.
- **Bar 1 (the filter, gates; set by Claude, confirmed by the user 2026-11-26).** Of the gists the filter kept, you reject at most 20% (not approved, which includes a flagged name) and 0 contain a real name. Reported with a Wilson 95% interval for the reject share. One day of headlines is a small sample: with 5 kept gists the interval for 1 rejection is about 4% to 62%, so a pass means "not clearly bad".
- **Bar 2 (funniness, gates; set by Claude, confirmed by the user 2026-11-26).** B wins at least 5 of 10 on (a); a tie is not a win. (b) is reported with a Wilson 95% interval, no bar. 10 pairs only detect large differences: if B is truly preferred 70% of the time it reaches 5 wins about 95% of the time, and under a coin flip it still reaches 5 about 62% of the time, so the gate is "not clearly worse".
- **Adopt** (flip `TONIGHT` on, same PR) only when both bars pass; a failed filter bar cannot be rescued by the pairs.
- **What is made up.** No `audience.yaml` value is involved (no sim room reward). The slot modes and rooms are seeded draws; the 20% and 5-of-10 bars are judgment calls, not measured. Your own approve and funny answers are the measurement.
- **Commands (user).** `python -m sim.tonight fetch --env-file backend/.env`, then `python -m sim.tonight run --env-file backend/.env`, then `python -m sim.tonight review`, then `python -m sim.tonight score --record`.

**Judgment calls, not measured.** A headline on 2 bits of 8 (bits 2 and 6); the paragraph's wording; the strict age for the gist check; allowing politics and health; the name-check allowlist; caching per day; the two bars.

**Result (Exp 35, 2026-11-26): the script says adopt; read honestly, bar 1 is inconclusive and bar 2 is a tie. Adopted as a judgment call (user).** Record: `reports/metrics/tonight.json`.
- What was tested is narrower than designed: the market fetch timed out at 10 s (no market line), the user typed no fact, and of 10 headlines 5 were dropped as disasters, 3 by the name check and 1 by the safety check, so all 10 pairs used one Thanksgiving gist.
- Bar 1: the user rejected 0 of 1 kept gist (Wilson 95% 0% to 79%), 0 names. The interval is far wider than the 20% gate, so by CLAUDE.md rule 4 it cannot decide; it is not a pass.
- Bar 2: B funnier in 5 of 10 (the bar). A tie, which meets a bar that only asked "not clearly worse".
- (b) feels like tonight: B 9 of 10 (Wilson 95% 60% to 98%), the one clear signal.
- Why adopt anyway (user's call): the operator approves every gist before the show, which is the real safety net; with nothing approved the show runs as before; and it was not less funny. `TONIGHT` is now on by default.
- Fixes after the run, same PR: fetch timeout 10 s to 30 s with one retry (FRED is slow, not down); every gist that does not reach the operator is kept with its reason in the day's file (Exp 35 could not say why its 3 name rejects were); and the pool grows from today's 10 top stories to today's top stories plus 14-day searches in technology, business, entertainment and sports (Google's top 8 of each by its own ranking, deduplicated, at most 40, condensed 15 per call). RSS has no read counts, so "popular" means Google's ranking (user asked for the stories followed over the last couple of weeks). No world or politics search, where the disasters were.
- Still open: bar 1 needs more days of fetches to mean anything (`fetch` alone, no pairs).
- **With material on as well (merged from main, D-117/D-118; material is off by default).** Judgment call, not measured: on bits 2 and 6 an approved headline is the topic and the bit draws no material, so it spends no topic or facts and writes no `material` row; other bits get material as usual. The generator id carries both suffixes (`gen-v5+mat-v1+tonight-v1:model`). With both off the writer is still called with two arguments, as `tests/test_predict_next.py` needs.
- **How far from a pass, in numbers.** Bar 1's sample note assumed about 5 kept gists and the day gave 1; its 95% interval runs 0% to 79%, a half-width of about 40 points against a 20% gate, so it is 2 times too wide to decide. Bar 2 was met at its minimum (5 of 10, so B was not funnier, only not worse). The market line and the typed line were never tested (none that day). On by default is the **user's judgment call, not measured**, and so are the fixes after the run (30 s timeout and retry, the 40-headline pool, the request headers): Exp 35 measured none of them.
- **Pre-merge review (2026-11-26, independent agent).** Re-derived: with tonight on and material off, the writer requests at HEAD equal those at 4efd7ce byte for byte (8 bits, 24 calls, fake writer); with `MATERIAL=1` and tonight off they equal `main` at 1403072; Exp 35's digest `6e588a6f` still matches, so the prompt, paragraph and slots are what was measured. Fixed: (1) a `keep` entry was trusted, so a player that Haiku listed as a team (or a holiday named after a person) was allowed mid-sentence in every joke of the show through `LiveSafety.allow_caps`, and an `occasion` entry re-allowed a lone "Black". Now only a sports name written as "the <name>" in its gist is kept, and holidays rely on `safety.OCCASIONS` alone. Judgment call, not measured: a team written without "the" ("Real Madrid") now sends its gist to the name check, and the next fetch may keep fewer gists. (2) The fetch could offer more than 10 gists, or one over 200 characters, which the start message refuses, so the show would not start; the panel caps ticks at 10 and the fetch drops overlong gists. (3) The `tonight` log row now carries the fetch errors. (4) The notebook lost Exp 35 whenever the gitignored local fetch was missing or replaced by a later day's; the record now stands unless the same day's material changed. Not changed: a person's name as the first word of a gist ("Musk unveils...") passes the cheap name check, as in jokes; the LLM safety check (rule 3, real people) and the operator are the gates there.

**Addendum, places and organisations (user, 2026-11-26: "keep the country names", then widened).** Before the Exp 35 rerun, the gist prompt and the rules let more names through, so the gist can say what the story is about. Judgment calls, not measured; the operator's approval of each gist is the last gate. `RULES_VERSION` is now `rules-v3` (the rules accept more, so verdicts stored under `rules-v2` can be found and redone; Exp 24 and the generator comparisons key on it). Exp 35's digest does not hash the gist prompt, so what the rerun measures is the new code, not the old record.
- **Places, fixed lists, masked as whole names before the capitals scan** (`safety.COUNTRIES` 211, `US_STATES` 51 with DC, `BIG_CITIES` 62; same method as `OCCASIONS`). "New Zealand", "South Africa", "Costa Rica", "New York City" pass; a lone "New", "South", "Saudi" or "Costa" mid-sentence is still read as a possible name. A possessive 's is fine. The rule applies to jokes and to gists alike (`safety._named_person`, `tonight.name_check`).
- **Left out of the lists on purpose.** Because the word is also a first name or a famous person: countries Jordan, Chad, Georgia; states Georgia and Virginia (lone words; "West Virginia" is listed) and Washington (lone; "Washington State" and "Washington DC" are listed); cities Austin, Florence, Paris, Orlando, Phoenix, Jackson, Charlotte, Victoria, Sydney, Dallas, Houston, Denver, Lincoln, Madison, Brooklyn, Milan, Geneva, Vienna, Chelsea, and San Jose ("jose" is in `FIRST_NAMES`). Because the protected-group rule reads a word in them: the Central African Republic and the United Arab Emirates ("UAE" is listed). A test checks that no listed place contains a `FIRST_NAMES` word and that each passes the rules at the strictest age. Not checked and kept: London, which is also a given name; it is not in `FIRST_NAMES`.
- **Country names only, never nationality words.** "French", "Canadians", "Chinese", "Japanese" stay blocked by the capitals rule (English was already allowed). Nationality is a protected attribute (invariant 1; the safety model's rule 2); a country as a place in the news is not about who is in the room.
- **Companies, products and organisations come from the writer's keep list, checked** (`tonight.kept_org`, types `org` and `product`). A kept name is accepted only if it is written verbatim in the gist, has at most 3 words, contains no `FIRST_NAMES` word and no title, and either is in `tonight.KNOWN_ORGS` (big tech and brands, agencies, bodies) or contains an org-marker word (`ORG_MARKERS`: Inc, Bank, University, Agency, Party, Labs...). A person labelled "org" has neither unless the name is dressed up, so "Musk", "Elon Musk" and "Taylor Swift" are dropped; the residue (a surname plus a marker word, "Musk Labs") is accepted and is what the operator must catch. An accepted name goes into the show's `allow_caps` like a team (tokens, per show, approved gists only). Tightened in the pre-merge review of D-120 (below): a marker org must also be in the headline, and it is allowed only whole.
- **The gist says what happened and why it is interesting or odd**, in plain words a stranger could follow without the headline, under 25 words (was 20). No second model call for vagueness; the user's rejections at `fetch` count against the 20% bar.
- **`fetch` question** now reads "Does it contain a name that should not be there?" (see D-120 for what that counts).

**Rerun (2026-11-26, HEAD 14f7bd0; places, checked organisations, public figures, "so what" gists, writer people paragraph): adopt, both bars met.** Record: `reports/metrics/tonight.json` (setup digest c386898b); the first run's numbers are above. TONIGHT was already on by default from the user's earlier call; it now also has a pass.

| Check | Result | Bar |
|---|---|---|
| Headlines fetched / dropped (death, disaster, crime, war) | 40 / 0, 5, 2, 0 | beside the bar |
| Rejected by the name check / by the safety check | 4 / 6 | beside the bar |
| Gists kept / approved | 23 / 21 | beside the bar |
| User rejected (likely range, one day) | 9% (2 of 23), 2% to 27% | at most 20% |
| Kept gists with a name that should not be there (flagged) | 0 | 0 |
| (a) Funnier: B wins | 5 of 10 (A 3, ties 2) | at least 5 |
| (b) More like tonight: B wins | 5 of 10 (A 4, ties 1), likely range 24% to 76% | beside the bar |
| Verdict | adopt | both bars |

Caveats, said plainly:
- Second attempt at the same bars. A second try raises the chance of a lucky pass.
- Funniness meets its bar exactly at the minimum (5 of 10 pairs). If B were no funnier than A (a coin flip), 5 or more wins out of 10 happens about 62% of the time, so this says "not clearly worse", not "funnier".
- The reject share is 9%, but its likely range reaches 27%, above the 20% bar. One day of headlines cannot rule out a reject rate above the bar.
- "More like tonight" fell from 9 of 10 (first run) to 5 of 10. The setups differ (names, places, "so what" summaries, 40 headlines instead of 10), and the run cannot say which caused it.
- The first run's filter bar counted names differently (D-120), so the two filter results are not comparable.
- The rerun used no approved public figure in any gist (the people list for the day is empty), so none of the 10 B bits could have named one: 0 of 10. The keep list held one name ("AI"), no place or organisation. The part of D-119 that adds names to jokes was not exercised by this run.

## <a id="d-120"></a>D-120 · 2026-11-26 — Public figures in the news may be named in tonight's gists and jokes (user)
The user chose "public figures in the news OK" over the other two options below (2026-11-26). Claude specified it, a Sonnet agent built it. Measured only by the Exp 35 rerun's filter bar and the user's own approvals; everything below is a judgment call, not measured.

**The problem.** D-119 kept every person out of the gists ("a tech CEO"). Many stories are only interesting because of who did what, so the gists came out vague.

| Option | For / against | Choice |
|---|---|---|
| People's names stay banned | Safest; vague gists | Rejected by the user |
| Public figures named in the news may be named | Gists are concrete; the story is about what they did or said. Risk: a model decides who is "public", a joke lands on a real person | **Chosen (user)** |
| Any names OK | Most concrete | Rejected: private people and people in the room |

**Design.**
- The gist writer may keep a real person's name only for a public figure (politician, official, CEO or founder, celebrity, athlete) when the story is about what they did or said, and marks it `type: "person"` in the keep list. A victim is never kept.
- Accepted only if (`tonight.kept_person`): the story's category is `none` (death, disaster, crime and war stay dropped, so a victim, defendant or casualty in them is never named); the name is 1 to 3 capitalized words with no title (an acronym or a role such as "a senator" is not a person); and it is written verbatim in BOTH the source headline and the gist, so a name the model invented cannot get in. The headline is passed into `parse_condensed`; without it no person is kept.
- The `fetch` and the operator panel show each gist's names next to it ("names: ..."), so approving a gist approves those names explicitly.
- Approved names join that show's `allow_people` only (`TonightContext`, per show; the next show starts from its own approved gists; an unapproved gist's names never reach it). A joke may name them by the full name or the last word when that word is not a first name ("Musk", never "Elon" alone, which could be anyone in the room). The name is masked before the capitals and first-name scan (`safety.mask_people`); the title rule ("Mr Musk") and every body, appearance and protected-group rule still apply, and a sentence naming an approved person counts as aimed at them, so "I read that X has an ugly nose" is refused by the rules and not only by the LLM.
- The safety model's rule against naming real people becomes: a real person may be named only if they are in tonight's approved names list, passed into the safety prompt (`safety.PEOPLE_RULE`); a line about them may be only about what they did or said, and is rejected if it touches their body, appearance, health, family or a protected attribute (race, ethnicity, nationality, religion, gender, sexual orientation, disability, age) or invents anything private. With an empty list the prompt is byte for byte what it was (tested), so a show with tonight off, or nothing approved, is unchanged. `safety.PROMPT_ID` is not bumped, on purpose: Exp 24 and the generator comparisons hash it, and the empty-list prompt did not change. Instead the names are part of the verdict's cache key (a verdict made with one list is never read by another), and `PEOPLE_PROMPT_ID = "safety-people-v1"` marks the wording.
- **The operator-typed tonight line keeps the old rule: no person names.** It is the likeliest place for the name of someone in the room (invariant 1). People in the room are never named, in any option.
- Log (invariant 6): the `tonight` row carries `allowed_people`; the day's file carries `people` per gist.

**Exp 35's filter bar changes meaning before the rerun.** "0 kept gists contain a real name" now means "0 gists with a name that should not be there": a person the user did not approve, a private person, or a name not in the headline. An approved public figure is not flagged. The `fetch` question was reworded to match, and it prints the public figures so the user can judge them. The 20% rejection bar is unchanged and now also absorbs a gist the user rejects because a name or the "so what" is wrong. Exp 35's earlier record (1 gist, 0 names) was judged under the old meaning; it is not comparable.

**Judgment calls, not measured.** Who counts as a public figure (the model's call, then the user's approval); the 3-word cap; the last-word form; allowing politics-adjacent people at all; that sentences naming an approved person are treated as aimed at them by the body rules.

**Pre-merge review (2026-11-26, independent agent).** Attacked with unit tests and scripts, no model calls. Re-derived: with an empty names list the safety prompt equals `main` at 1403072 for every age, and `safety.PROMPT_ID` is unchanged; not bumping it is sound because verdicts live only in the in-memory cache, keyed by the names list, and nothing persisted is keyed on the people prompt. Per-show scope holds: `make_stack` builds a new `LiveSafety` per show, and cached lines from earlier shows are re-screened with this show's names. Fixed:
1. **A surname dressed as an org unlocked the surname alone.** "Musk Labs", "Swift Inc" or "Bezos Corp" passed `kept_org`, and `caps_tokens` split it, so "Musk" alone was allowed in every joke of the show without the people rules. Now a marker org must be verbatim in the source headline (a made-up one cannot get in), and `caps_tokens` keeps it whole; `safety.mask_phrases` lets the whole name through and a lone "Musk" is still read as a name. Known orgs and teams still allow their words.
2. **A lone first name could be kept as a public figure.** "Taylor" or "Jordan" as a one-word person would have unlocked anyone in the room with that name. `kept_person` and `people_forms` now drop a one-word name in `FIRST_NAMES`.
3. **No backstop when the model labels a dark story "none".** `tonight.DARK` (died, killed, arrested, police, crash, hospitalized, diagnosed, war, attack...) in the headline or gist now keeps no person's name. Over-cautious on purpose: a gist like that then fails the name check and is not offered. Judgment call, not measured.
4. **Health, family and age had no rule, and looks only counted in the same sentence.** A line naming an approved person is now refused by the rules for a family, health or age word (`safety.PERSONAL`: wife, kids, rehab, cancer, birthday...), and a looks word anywhere in that line counts as aimed at them, not only in the same sentence. Lines that name no approved person are unchanged.
5. **Places.** Holland (a famous actor's surname; the Netherlands is listed) and Niger (a voice can make it sound like a slur) are out of `COUNTRIES`. No listed place contains a `FIRST_NAMES` word; lone "New", "South", "San" and nationality words are still caught (tested). Kept, judgment call: India, Israel, London, Berlin, Wales, Montana, Cuba and Indiana are also names or surnames of someone; the operator and the LLM check are the gates.
6. **Smaller.** The `fetch` question said "the public figures shown above" but none were shown; it now prints them. The `tonight` log row names `people_prompt` when names were approved (invariant 6). A test headline about a real singer dying was replaced with a made-up name.
Not changed: a surname as the first word of a sentence still passes the rules (as before D-120; the LLM check is the gate); a last word that is also a nationality ("Cuban" in a name) is masked for that show; the joke writer's frozen prompt still says "no real or named people" (superseded for approved names by the writer paragraph below).

**Writer paragraph for approved public figures (user, 2026-11-26).** With the frozen "no real or named people" rule, approved names almost never reached a joke. `tonight.PEOPLE_PARAGRAPH` is now appended to the writer's system prompt, after the tonight paragraph, **only when the show's approved people list is non-empty**: "Tonight you may name these public figures from the news: <names>. Joke only about what they did or said in tonight's story. Never their body, looks, health, family, age or any protected group. Name no one else." `PROMPTS` stay frozen (gen-v4 and gen-v5 hash pins unchanged). With an empty list the request is byte for byte what it was (tested, and diffed against HEAD 9ab290e for a fake-writer request with tonight off and with tonight on and no people). The generator id gets `+people-v1` only when the paragraph is sent, so logs say which bits could name people; the `tonight` row logs the names offered (`allowed_people`) and `people_writer`. Exp 35's arm B sends it when the gist carries approved people, as the show does, and the digest covers the paragraph, its suffix and the gist's people. Note: the tonight paragraph still says "name nobody" for the headline topic; the later people paragraph is the exception, left unedited so the no-people request stays byte-identical. Judgment call, wording not measured; the safety model's `PEOPLE_RULE` and the rules remain the gate.

**Addendum to D-119 places (user's call, 2026-11-26).** The user chose to keep all current places (India, Israel, London, Berlin, Wales, Montana, Cuba, Indiana) even though each is also a name or surname.

**Rerun (2026-11-26, HEAD 14f7bd0; places, checked organisations, public figures, "so what" gists, writer people paragraph): adopt, both bars met.** Record: `reports/metrics/tonight.json` (setup digest c386898b); the first run's numbers are above. TONIGHT was already on by default from the user's earlier call; it now also has a pass.

| Check | Result | Bar |
|---|---|---|
| Headlines fetched / dropped (death, disaster, crime, war) | 40 / 0, 5, 2, 0 | beside the bar |
| Rejected by the name check / by the safety check | 4 / 6 | beside the bar |
| Gists kept / approved | 23 / 21 | beside the bar |
| User rejected (likely range, one day) | 9% (2 of 23), 2% to 27% | at most 20% |
| Kept gists with a name that should not be there (flagged) | 0 | 0 |
| (a) Funnier: B wins | 5 of 10 (A 3, ties 2) | at least 5 |
| (b) More like tonight: B wins | 5 of 10 (A 4, ties 1), likely range 24% to 76% | beside the bar |
| Verdict | adopt | both bars |

Caveats, said plainly:
- Second attempt at the same bars. A second try raises the chance of a lucky pass.
- Funniness meets its bar exactly at the minimum (5 of 10 pairs). If B were no funnier than A (a coin flip), 5 or more wins out of 10 happens about 62% of the time, so this says "not clearly worse", not "funnier".
- The reject share is 9%, but its likely range reaches 27%, above the 20% bar. One day of headlines cannot rule out a reject rate above the bar.
- "More like tonight" fell from 9 of 10 (first run) to 5 of 10. The setups differ (names, places, "so what" summaries, 40 headlines instead of 10), and the run cannot say which caused it.
- The first run's filter bar counted names differently (D-120), so the two filter results are not comparable.
- D-120's public-figure rule is covered by the filter bar only (0 flagged). No gist had an approved public figure on this day, so 0 of the 10 B bits named one, and the rule's effect on jokes is unmeasured.

## <a id="d-121"></a>D-121 · 2026-11-26 — DPO pairs: write them in the background with the live writer, rank 120 of them yourself, and gate the judge on 60 (Exp 36) (user)
Decided by the user, 2026-11-26, before any pair is written or ranked. Carries out D-102 (the gate) and D-110 (a bit is 3 parallel threads, so any two are a valid pair). **Half the prompts carry tonight's context and half do not** (user's choice). Judge labelling of the training split and DPO training wait for the gate.

**Choice.** One PR with two tools and the gate: (a) `python -m sim.dpo_pairs write` writes bits in the background with the live writer, (b) `rank` is the 120-pair ranking tool of D-102, (c) `judge` and `score --record` are Exp 36. All in `sim/dpo_pairs.py`; generated text, the pool and your answers live under `data/dpo/` (gitignored); `reports/metrics/dpo_judge_gate.json` holds numbers only.

**Which judge (the user's choice, 2026-11-26): Claude Haiku 5.5, thinking off.** D-102 names "the D-95 judge", a few-shot ranker on a local Qwen; it was never built or run (D-102's result: "the D-95 judge was not used"). D-114's judge v2 is not its successor: it is Haiku 5.5 deciding whether five lines are one bit or separate jokes, and it never says which of two threads is funnier. So no pair judge existed. The judge is the client D-114 uses (`thread_coherence.make_judge_client`), taught by your 60 teach pairs as examples in its system message, replying `{"funnier": "A"|"B"}`. **Local Qwen rejected by the user:** weaker, shares the GPU with the writer, and likelier to fail the gate with no time left to retry. The writer stays the open-weights model being trained; the judge is frozen, so it does not need to be open. Cost: D-95's own leave-one-out bars were never measured for any judge and are not run here; the 30 Exp 24 votes are not added as examples (a three-way ballot, another shape). The gate below is the test that decides.

**Prompt pool (`python -m sim.dpo_pairs pool`, frozen in `data/dpo/pool.json`, seeds `dpo-pool-v1` and `dpo-rank-v1`).** Requests are built the way the show builds them: `ThreadGenerator`, gen-v5 (`PROMPT_ID`), material off (the live default), `set_tonight` for the tonight half, so the request is the show's (a test compares them at bit 2 and at a bit with no topic, with and without an approved public figure). The bit number runs 0 to 7 in rotation; a bit after the first has that many premises already told (up to 4, from a new placeholder list). Modes rotate (every mode in every cell), rooms are seeded (venue, energy, age; all values covered). Two disjoint splits, fixed before any data:

| Split | Prompts | Tonight half | Plain half | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ranking | 240 | 120 | 120 | your 120 pairs (one pair per bit); 60 teach, 60 test, assigned per prompt now |
| Training | 800 | 400 | 400 | DPO later; up to 3 pairs per bit once the judge passes |

No prompt in either split equals one of Exp 24's ballot (and spares), Exp 26, 27 (all 100) or 30, and the two splits share none: `assert_disjoint` checks the full request key (mode, room, premises told, half, day, gist; not the bit number, which is not in the request) and a test breaks it on purpose. Two plain first bits (nothing told) had no free room left, because Exp 24 and 26 already used most of the 27 rooms per mode, and became second bits (`bumped_first_bits`). **Tonight half:** the day's context (market line, typed line if any) is background for every bit, and an approved gist is the topic on bits 2 and 6 (30 of the 120 ranking prompts, 100 of the 400 training ones), gists taken round-robin from a seeded shuffle of the approved list. **Limitation: there is one day.** The stored fetches hold 2026-11-26 only (21 approved gists; the older run 1 fetch of the same day, 1 gist, is ignored as superseded), so each gist is reused by about 6 prompts across both splits (1 to 2 in the ranking split), in different modes and rooms, the market line is the same for all, and no public figure was approved (`people` is empty). **Before judge labelling, rebuild the training pool with 3 or more days of approved fetches** (user's decision): run `python -m sim.tonight fetch` on more days, then `python -m sim.dpo_pairs pool --split train --rebuild`. It rebuilds only the training split around the frozen ranking split (still disjoint from it and from the earlier experiments), moves any written training bits aside (their ids name other prompts now), and refuses if any training bit has been labelled (`data/dpo/labels/`) or a day the ranking split used is missing or changed. Training bits can be written on one day meanwhile, but they are discarded by that rebuild.

**Writer.** One prompt is one bit is 3 parallel calls on the live writer (bonsai on Ollama, `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=3`, warmed up and checked on the GPU like a show), at most one bit at a time, then the show's safety check (rules plus Haiku, with the day's names and approved people allowed for the tonight half) on every line. A thread with any rejected line is recorded (`safe: false`, reasons) and never enters a pair. A bit whose safety layer itself failed is re-checked on the next run, not regenerated; a bit with no thread is written again on the next run, and 3 such bits in a row stop the writer (Ollama down; judgment call, not measured). The safety check's allow lists are set for every bit (the day's for tonight, none for plain). Each record keeps the request, generator id, timing, bad replies, lost threads, a digest of the request and writer settings, and the rules version (invariant 6). Finished bits are never rewritten; files are replaced atomically, so Ctrl-C leaves whole files only. Expect about 8 s a bit: 240 ranking bits in about 35 minutes, 800 training bits in about 2 hours. **Do not run it during a live show:** it shares the GPU and Ollama with the show's writer, and a show's 12 s bit deadline would pay for it.

**Top-up (added 2026-11-26, after the 240 ranking bits were written).** Why: the show's safety check rejected about 40% of threads, so only 150 of the 240 ranking bits had 2 or more clean threads (a pair needs 2). Usable bits per cell (role, half): teach/plain 33, teach/tonight 37, test/plain 42, test/tonight 38, but each cell needs 30 decided pairs and ties and skips are replaced from the same cell, so teach/plain could absorb only 3 ties or skips before `next_pair` ran out. What: `python -m sim.dpo_pairs topup --split ranking` writes one more batch of 3 threads for every written bit with fewer than 2 clean threads, with the byte-identical request stored in the record (it rebuilds the request and stops if its text, generator id or digest differs from the stored one), runs the safety check with that bit's allow lists, and appends the threads (each carries its batch number; the record keeps each top-up's time and digest). Up to 2 extra batches per bit (judgment call, not measured). Bits with 2 or more clean threads are skipped, so a pair already shown keeps its pair. Same stop as `write` (3 empty in a row), atomic writes, resumable. Effect on validity: both threads of a pair still come from one request, so it is a valid DPO pair. Selection effect, not measured: prompts whose writing the safety check rejects often get more draws, so topped-up bits lean toward prompts the writer finds harder to keep safe. **Lead, not changed here:** the live show itself discards about 40% of threads by rules; the top rule reasons are "body_or_appearance:old/face/short/small/look like a" and "teens_alcohol:bartender", worth a later look for false positives. The safety rules are not changed in this decision.

**Ranking tool.** You see two clean threads of one ranking bit, 5 lines each, sides random by seed, no premise, no label. Answer 1, 2, t (tie), s (skip) or q; every answer is saved. Ties and skips do not count and the prompt is replaced by the next one of its cell, never shown again; both counts are recorded. The cells are teach and test, each for the tonight and the plain half: 30 decided pairs in each, so 60 teach, 60 test, and 30 of each tonight half in both. Roles are assigned to prompts by seed before any answer, so no answer can move a pair between teach and test; ranking prompts that share a headline gist share a role, so no test pair has a teach example's topic. The next question comes from a cell with the fewest decided pairs, so a short sitting stays balanced (`rank --limit 20`); equal cells are drawn by seed and the progress line shows only the total, so you cannot tell which questions are test pairs (a tie or skip on a known test pair would pick easy test pairs). Expect about 35 s a pair, about 70 minutes for 120.

**Exp 36, the judge gate (D-102's bar for the pairs it can label; the rule for inconsistent pairs is the user's, 2026-11-26, set before any data).** The judge is taught with the 60 teach pairs (each as you saw it, seeded order) and asked on each of the 60 test pairs in both orders (A is your left thread, then swapped): 120 calls, cached per call and resumable. A test pair is never in the teach prompt (an assertion and a test). **A test pair whose two orders name different threads gets no label.** Let c be the test pairs the judge answered the same way in both orders and a those that agree with you. **Pass iff c >= 30 (at least half of the 60) AND the Wilson 95% lower bound of a/c is above 50%.** The minimum a that clears the bound is 21 of c = 30, 30 of c = 45 and 38 of c = 60 (computed; at c = 29 the gate fails on coverage whatever a is). Reported: a, c, the share of pairs whose orders disagree, agreement per tonight half. Beside the bar, never deciding: the strict count (a of all 60), the rate with a disagreeing pair earning half (D-95's convention, `agree_flip_half_credit`), and agreement in a single order. A missing judge reply makes the verdict `incomplete`, never a fail. **Training labeller contract (later PR):** a training pair is labelled in both orders like a test pair, and a pair whose orders disagree is dropped from DPO, not labelled by a guess.

**Power of this rule (computed, exact binomial).** Assumption: the judge is right with probability p in each order, the two orders' errors independent, no position preference, 60 test pairs. Then a pair is consistent with probability p^2 + (1-p)^2 and the judge agrees with you on it with probability p^2 / (p^2 + (1-p)^2). Chance of passing: **50% right: 1.4%; 65%: 74%; 70%: 91%; 75%: 98%; 80%: 99.9%** (at 65% about 33 pairs are consistent, 78% of them agreeing). D-102's table counted one verdict per pair and gave 66% / 90% / 98%, so this rule costs little. The assumption is generous in one way (a real judge's two orders are correlated, which raises c) and harsh in another (a judge with a position preference loses consistent pairs), so a fail may mean position bias: the disagreement share is reported for that.

**Options and trade-offs.**
| Choice | Options | Chosen, and why |
|---|---|---|
| Prompts | (a) reuse Exp 24 or 27 prompts: their threads were already judged or ranked, so leakage. (b) a new seeded pool | (b). Disjointness is asserted, not hoped for |
| Pairs per bit | (a) all 3 pairs of a bit: more pairs from fewer prompts, but pairs of one bit share a prompt and are correlated. (b) one pair per bit | (b) for the ranking and the gate, so the 60 test pairs are 60 independent prompts. The training split may use all 3 later |
| Teach or test | (a) first 60 answers teach, the rest test: a drift in your taste or tiredness lands on one side. (b) assigned per prompt by seed before any answer | (b) |
| Ties and skips | (a) keep a tie as a half agreement: a tie is not a pair a DPO run can use. (b) replace them | (b), as asked; counts recorded |
| Tonight half | all, none, or half | Half, the user's call: tonight's context is on in the show, so the judge must also agree on its bits, and the plain half checks the base writer |
| Judge | (a) the D-95 design on a local Qwen: weaker, shares the GPU with the writer, likelier to fail the gate with no time to retry. (b) Haiku 5.5, frozen, closed weights | (b), the user's choice: the judge is frozen, so it need not be open |
| Inconsistent pairs | (a) count as a miss (strict): punishes position bias as wrong taste. (b) half credit: no clean pass rule. (c) no label, gate on the consistent pairs with a coverage floor | (c), the user's rule: a pair the judge flips on carries no preference, so it is dropped from DPO too |
| Training pool and days | (a) wait for more days. (b) build now on one day, rebuild only the training split with 3+ days before labelling | (b): the ranking split is frozen once built; `pool --split train --rebuild` is refused once any training bit is labelled |

**Judgment calls, not measured.** Pool sizes (240 and 800); the 60/60 and 30/30 split (D-102); one pair per bit; the 30-pair coverage floor (half of 60); 60 pairs as the judge's whole teaching set; premises already told come from a placeholder list, as in Exp 27; non-topic tonight bits carry only the market line (as in a show); the writer runs outside a show, with nobody speaking, so its timing is not what a show sees. **Not checked:** whether the frozen judge stays aligned with your taste as DPO moves the model away from bonsai's starting outputs (reward hacking), as D-102 says; no check is planned during training. Also not checked: whether 120 pairs ranked over several sittings drift.

**What is made up:** nothing from `audience.yaml`; no sim room reward is read.

**Commands (you run all of these; the PR runs none).**
```
python -m sim.dpo_pairs pool                                            # once; reads data/tonight/**/fetch.json (junction data/tonight if it is in another checkout)
python -m sim.dpo_pairs write --split ranking --env-file backend/.env   # first, in the background; resumable; 240 bits
python -m sim.dpo_pairs topup --split ranking --env-file backend/.env   # after the write; bits with fewer than 2 clean threads get up to 2 more batches; resumable
python -m sim.dpo_pairs rank --limit 20                                 # sittings; prints "N of 120 done"
python -m sim.dpo_pairs write --split train --env-file backend/.env     # can run while you rank; --n N bounds a sitting
python -m sim.dpo_pairs rerank [--limit N]                              # after 120 are decided; 20 pairs again, reported only; resumable
python -m sim.dpo_pairs judge --env-file backend/.env                   # after 120 are decided; 120 Haiku calls
python -m sim.dpo_pairs score --record                                  # writes reports/metrics/dpo_judge_gate.json
python -m sim.dpo_pairs pool --split train --rebuild                    # later, with 3+ days stored, before any judge labelling
```
